Browse All / EFEMP2-Related Cutis Laxa

EFEMP2-Related Cutis Laxa

Mendelian MONDO:0013754 Pathograph 66 Show in embeddings browser hereditary disease autosomal recessive cutis laxa type 1

EFEMP2-related cutis laxa (autosomal recessive cutis laxa type 1B, ARCL1B) is a rare congenital connective tissue disorder caused by biallelic loss-of-function variants in EFEMP2, which encodes the short fibulin fibulin-4. Fibulin-4 binds tropoelastin and the propeptide of lysyl oxidase (LOX), is needed for copper loading and proteolytic maturation of LOX, and so supports both elastin and collagen cross-linking; in the aortic wall it is also needed for smooth muscle cell maturation and for the connections between elastic laminae and smooth muscle cells. The dominant clinical problem is a vasculopathy of the large elastic arteries: ascending aortic and aortic root aneurysm, elongation and tortuosity of the aorta, pulmonary arteries and their branches, and branch-vessel stenosis, with aortic isthmus hypoplasia in the most severe infants. Cutis laxa, emphysema, diaphragmatic and inguinal hernias, joint laxity, arachnodactyly, retrognathia and, in the severest cases, prenatal fractures are variable. Severity ranges from fetal or infantile death to isolated aortopathy found on family screening in adulthood. Mouse models show increased aortic TGF-beta signalling, an ACE and angiotensin II driven aneurysm pathway initiated by abnormal smooth muscle mechanosensing, and collagen fibril defects. Management is surveillance, angiotensin receptor or beta-blockade, and surgical replacement of the aneurysmal aorta.

Inheritance

1
Autosomal recessive inheritance HP:0000007
Biallelic EFEMP2 variants, homozygous in most reported families, many of which are consanguineous, or compound heterozygous. Heterozygous carriers are asymptomatic. Each sib of an affected individual has a 25% chance of being affected. Expressivity varies within families.
Autosomal recessive inheritance

Recurrence and carrier risks; carriers are asymptomatic.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

6
Is increased TGF-beta signalling in the fibulin-4 deficient aorta a driver of medial degeneration and aneurysm, or a consequence of the disordered matrix that marks damage without causing it?
CONTROVERSY OPEN controversy_efemp2_tgfbeta_driver_or_consequence
Attached to
pathophysiology#Increased TGF-beta Signaling in the Aortic Wall pathophysiology#Aortic Medial Degeneration treatments#Angiotensin Receptor Blockade for Aortic Root Dilatation
The rise is well documented on both sides of the species divide: pSmad2 and CTGF are increased in patient aortic and lung tissue, and the mouse transcriptome, Smad2 phosphorylation and TGF-beta2 levels all point the same way. What has never been shown is that blocking TGF-beta prevents the aneurysm. The strongest argument against a required role is that smooth muscle cells lacking fibulin-4 entirely do not raise TGF-beta activation, yet that genotype still produces thoracic aortic aneurysm, so the pathway cannot be the only route. The practical stake is that angiotensin receptor blockade is offered partly on a TGF-beta rationale, and losartan's benefit in the mouse is partial, early-window dependent and does not restore wall structure. Pending resolution, the conforms_to edge to the module's TGF-beta hub is declared with mechanism_confidence PROVISIONAL.
Proposed experiments
Timed TGF-beta neutralisation in fibulin-4 deficient mouse aorta
exp_efemp2_tgfbeta_blockade_timed
Neutralise TGF-beta, or delete Smad2 or the type II receptor in smooth muscle cells, in both the Fbln4 smooth muscle knockout and the Fibulin-4 hypomorph, starting inside and outside the first postnatal month, and score aneurysm incidence, medial histology and wall mechanics against losartan as a positive control. The two genotypes differ in whether TGF-beta activation rises at all, so running both separates a required pathway from a marker of matrix damage.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:20389311 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"we provide the first evidence for the involvement of altered TGFbeta signaling in the pathogenesis of FBLN4 mutations in humans"
The authors claim pathogenic involvement, but their data are immunostaining and immunoblotting, which show association rather than causation.
PMID:30844428 REFUTE PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"Since both mouse models show thoracic aortic aneurysm formation, we conclude that not only hampered TGFβ signaling, but also SMA cytoskeleton dynamics play an important role in aortic aneurysmal disease."
The authors' own conclusion that TGF-beta dysregulation is not sufficient to explain aneurysm formation in fibulin-4 deficiency.
How far do the fibulin-4 mouse models represent the human disease, particularly for the skeletal phenotype, where patients have prenatal fractures but null mice have an essentially normal skeleton?
HUMAN MODEL MISMATCH OPEN gap_efemp2_mouse_human_mismatch
Attached to
pathophysiology#Deficient Collagen Cross-Linking pathophysiology#Abnormal Collagen Fibril Assembly pathophysiology#PAR1 Activation in Aortic Smooth Muscle Cells phenotypes#Multiple prenatal fractures
Almost everything in this entry downstream of the elastic fiber defect rests on mouse evidence; in patients only reduced matrix fibulin-4, abnormal elastic fibers, cystic medial degeneration and increased pSmad2 and CTGF staining have been shown directly. The clearest mismatch is bone. Human truncating and founder genotypes produce innumerable prenatal fractures severe enough to be read as osteogenesis imperfecta type IIC, whereas fibulin-4 null mice have no skeletal morphological abnormality beyond forelimb contractures and thick collagen fibrils, despite the same biochemical lesion of reduced lysyl oxidase processing and collagen cross-linking. The mechanosensing arm has a second kind of gap: the PAR1-Egr1-ACE-Thbs1 cascade is established in mouse aorta and the proteins are raised in human thoracic aortic aneurysm tissue, but that human tissue was not from EFEMP2 patients. Separately, the main hypomorphic line arose from targeting the neighbouring Mus81 gene, so it is a dosage model and not a patient allele; the E57K knock-in is the only model of a human variant.
Proposed experiments
Skeletal phenotyping of patient-allele knock-in mice against human bone
exp_efemp2_skeletal_phenotyping_knockin
Phenotype the bone of E57K knock-in mice and of new knock-ins carrying the lethal human alleles p.Asp203Ala and p.Cys213Ter with microCT, biomechanical testing and collagen cross-link analysis, and compare against bone or skin fibroblasts from patients with the matching genotypes. This tests whether the absent murine fracture phenotype reflects a species difference in skeletal loading and collagen cross-link chemistry or simply an allele difference.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:26690653 REFUTE PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"No morphological abnormalities were found in the skeleton of Fbln4(-/-) mice as compared to wild type littermates except forelimb contractures as well as unusually thick collagen fibrils."
The mouse skeleton is spared the fractures that dominate severe human cases, although the underlying cross-linking lesion is present.
PMID:22070778 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"The radiographic findings were initially thought to represent osteogenesis imperfecta type IIC."
The severity of the human skeletal phenotype that the mouse does not reproduce.
PMID:17293478 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Fibulin-4 knockdown mice with a hypomorphic expression allele arose from targeted disruption of the adjacent Mus81 endonuclease gene."
The workhorse hypomorph is a dosage model of incidental origin, not a model of any patient genotype.
Does fibulin-4 loss actually reduce elastin cross-linking in the artery wall? Null mouse tissue has largely diminished elastin cross-links, but the E57K knock-in and the smooth muscle knockout both have normal arterial elastin cross-linking while still developing aneurysm.
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN gap_efemp2_elastin_crosslink_conflict
Attached to
pathophysiology#Deficient Elastin Cross-Linking pathophysiology#Reduced Lysyl Oxidase Activity pathophysiology#Impaired Lysyl Oxidase Recruitment onto Tropoelastin pathophysiology#Conduit Artery Elastic Lamina Fragmentation
The lysyl oxidase tethering model predicts that reduced fibulin-4 lowers elastin cross-linking everywhere it acts. Desmosine analysis of complete null tissue agrees. Two later models disagree for the artery: in the E57K knock-in, elastin cross-linking and total elastin content were unchanged in large and small arteries even though elastic fiber architecture was abnormal and aneurysms formed, and the authors say in as many words that this calls the tethering role into question; in the smooth muscle knockout, collagen cross-linking fell but elastin cross-linking was unaffected and mature lysyl oxidase was maintained. The results are not strictly incompatible, since they differ in allele, severity, tissue and assay, and the E57K skin does have reduced desmosine while its arteries do not. But the published positions cannot all be true of the aorta at once, and the resolution decides whether the aortic phenotype is a cross-linking disease at all or an elastic fiber architecture and mechanosensing disease. This entry therefore keeps the cross-linking arm pointed at skin, lung and bone and routes the aortic chain through the lamina-smooth muscle connection and mechanosensing nodes instead.
Proposed experiments
Harmonised desmosine and collagen cross-link quantification across fibulin-4 alleles and tissues
exp_efemp2_crosslink_assay_harmonisation
Measure desmosine, isodesmosine and lysyl pyridinoline cross-links by the same mass spectrometry protocol, in aorta, skin, lung, tendon and bone, across the null, hypomorph, smooth muscle knockout and E57K knock-in at matched ages, and in patient skin and aortic tissue where available. Report absolute cross-link density per unit elastin and collagen rather than relative change, so that allele, tissue and assay are no longer confounded.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:16478991 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Desmosine analysis indicated that elastin cross-links in fibulin-4-/- tissues were largely diminished."
The founding observation for reduced elastin cross-linking, in complete null tissue.
PMID:28508064 REFUTE PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Normal levels of elastin cross-links in mutant tissue call into question FBLN4's suggested role in mediating lysyl oxidase-elastin interactions."
The authors explicitly challenge the tethering model on the basis of normal arterial elastin cross-links in a patient-allele knock-in.
PMID:26220971 REFUTE PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"collagen cross-linking was diminished in SMKO aortas; however, elastin cross-linking was unaffected and the level of mature LOX was maintained to that of wild-type aortas"
Dissociates the collagen and elastin arms in the aorta, with mature lysyl oxidase preserved.
+ 1 more reference
What predicts severity in EFEMP2 disease? Truncating alleles and the p.Asp203Ala founder genotype are usually lethal in infancy while p.Glu161Lys homozygotes reach adulthood, but no formal correlation study exists and expressivity varies within single families.
OPEN QUESTION OPEN open_question_efemp2_genotype_phenotype
Attached to
genetic#EFEMP2 phenotypes#Cutis laxa phenotypes#Aortic isthmus hypoplasia
Cohort-level patterns are suggestive. Truncating genotypes appear in fetal and perinatally lethal cases; p.Asp203Ala homozygosity killed 17 of 21 infants at a median age of four months; p.Glu161Lys homozygosity gives severe aortic aneurysm with normal skin and survivors found incidentally at 17 and 20 years. Against a simple allele-severity rule, intrafamilial variability was explicit in the Saudi families, where relatives sharing one homozygous genotype ranged from cyanosis at three months to an asymptomatic adult, and the functional work shows that different missense alleles fail in different ways, losing secretion, stability, lysyl oxidase dependent cross-linking, or binding to individual partners, which is not a single severity axis. The one feature with reported prognostic weight is anatomical rather than molecular: isthmic hypoplasia correlated with death at or before four months. Because the alleles are founder alleles, population background and modifier genes are confounded with genotype in every cohort published so far.
Proposed experiments
Multi-centre EFEMP2 genotype-phenotype registry with standardised imaging
exp_efemp2_genotype_phenotype_registry
Pool reported and unreported EFEMP2 families into one registry recording genotype, aortic root and ascending aorta Z-scores at matched ages, presence of isthmic hypoplasia, skin involvement, fractures and age and cause of death, then test allele class, residual secreted fibulin-4 in patient fibroblasts, and isthmic hypoplasia as competing predictors of survival. Including within-family pairs is essential, since intrafamilial variability is the main evidence against a genotype-only model.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:22440127 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"In the affected subjects, the clinical presentation ranged from sweating and cyanosis at 3 months of age to incidental findings in an asymptomatic adult."
One homozygous genotype spans most of the clinical range, which limits how much genotype alone can predict.
PMID:27339457 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"The substitutions E126K and C267Y impaired secretion of the protein, but not mRNA synthesis."
Missense alleles fail by different molecular routes, so severity is unlikely to follow a single ordering.
How should the aortopathy be managed? No human trial exists, the mouse evidence favours angiotensin blockade over beta-blockade and only inside an early window, and neither drug restores the aortic wall, so when and in whom to operate is unsettled.
OPEN QUESTION OPEN open_question_efemp2_aneurysm_management
Attached to
treatments#Angiotensin Receptor Blockade for Aortic Root Dilatation treatments#Beta-Blockade for Aortic Root Dilatation treatments#Aortic Aneurysm Replacement Surgery treatments#Cardiovascular and Pulmonary Surveillance
GeneReviews states only that beta-blockers or angiotensin receptor inhibitors can be considered, which is as far as the human evidence reaches. The preclinical data are sharper and less comfortable: losartan or captopril prevented aneurysm completely in the fibulin-4 smooth muscle knockout but only when given during the first postnatal month, and without reversing the altered mechanics of the wall; in adult hypomorphs losartan reduced aneurysm size and improved survival while aliskiren and propranolol did neither, and no drug rescued elastic fiber fragmentation in established aneurysms. If a comparable critical window exists in patients it would fall in infancy, before most patients are diagnosed, and in the founder cohort median presentation was at 1.5 months with most deaths by four months. Surgical thresholds are borrowed from other aortopathies, operations are reported from 19 months of age onward, and reoperation for conduit stenosis or a new aneurysm is reported, so neither the medical nor the surgical timing question has a disorder-specific answer.
Proposed experiments
Prospective early-initiation angiotensin receptor blocker cohort with imaging endpoints
exp_efemp2_early_arb_trial
In founder populations where the genotype can be identified at or before birth by cascade or prenatal testing, start an angiotensin receptor blocker in the neonatal period in consenting families and compare aortic root and ascending aorta Z-score trajectories, isthmic anatomy and survival against genotype-matched historical and concurrent controls started later. A randomised trial is unlikely to be feasible at this prevalence, so a prospective genotype-matched cohort with prespecified imaging endpoints is the realistic design.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:21563328 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Treatment of aortic root dilatation with beta-blockers or angiotensin receptor inhibitors can be considered."
The authoritative human recommendation is permissive, not directive, which is itself the measure of the evidence gap.
PMID:23636094 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"even though the altered mechanical properties of blood vessel walls were not reversed by the pharmacological treatment"
Prevention of aneurysm without repair of the wall, which is why drug treatment does not remove the need for surveillance or surgery.
PMID:26828783 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Although losartan and aliskiren reduced hemodynamic stress and increased renin similarly, only losartan increased survival."
The benefit is not explained by load reduction alone, so drug choice within the class matters and cannot be inferred from blood pressure.
Several reported features have no proposed mechanism in this entry: why some arterial segments narrow while others dilate, why the aortic isthmus is specifically hypoplastic, and how corneal thinning, baroreflex failure with cerebral vasospasm, fetal overgrowth and microcephaly arise from fibulin-4 loss.
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN gap_efemp2_unexplained_features
Attached to
phenotypes#Arterial stenosis phenotypes#Aortic isthmus hypoplasia pathophysiology#Conduit Artery Elastic Lamina Fragmentation
The entry routes stenosis and isthmic hypoplasia from the conduit artery lamina defect with indirect edges and unknown intermediates, which is honest but uninformative: a single elastic fiber lesion is being asked to explain both dilation and narrowing in the same aorta, and the earliest abnormality in null embryos is narrowing of the descending aorta rather than dilation anywhere. Isthmic hypoplasia carries real prognostic weight, so its developmental basis is worth resolving rather than recording. Separately, single-case features are not modelled here at all because one report each supports them and no mechanism has been proposed: keratoglobus with corneal rupture in a 38-year-old, recurrent thunderclap headache with baroreflex failure and cerebral vasospasm in a child, and fetal overgrowth with microcephaly in the lethal p.Cys267Tyr case. Baroreflex failure is the most mechanistically suggestive of these, since baroreceptors sit in the elastic wall of the carotid sinus and aortic arch and would be expected to misreport strain in a disorganised elastic lamina, but that link has been proposed rather than tested.
Proposed experiments
Segmental developmental mapping of the fibulin-4 deficient aorta
exp_efemp2_segmental_aorta_development
Follow the fibulin-4 null and E57K embryonic aorta from E11.5 through birth with light-sheet imaging and segment-resolved single-cell transcriptomics, separating neural crest derived from somite derived and second heart field derived smooth muscle along the arch and isthmus, and ask whether narrowing segments differ from dilating segments in lineage, elastic lamina number, or local haemodynamic load. The same sections should be scored for baroreceptor afferent innervation of the carotid sinus and arch to test whether the baroreflex phenotype has a structural correlate.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:16478991 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"The earliest abnormality noted was a uniformly narrowing of the descending aorta in fibulin-4-/- embryos at embryonic day 12.5 (E12.5)."
Narrowing precedes tortuosity and aneurysm in development, so stenosis is not simply a late remodelling response to dilation elsewhere.
PMID:24733866 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"we present the first case of a child with recurrent thunderclap headache and cerebral vasospasm with baroreflex failure secondary to vascular complications of a rare genetic connective tissue disorder"
A single case attributing baroreflex failure to the arteriopathy, with no mechanistic test of the attribution.
PMID:31194159 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"We believe that this is the first reported case of keratoglobus associated with cutis laxa."
Corneal thinning in an adult EFEMP2 patient, reported once and not otherwise explained.

Pathophysiology

26
Biallelic EFEMP2 Loss-of-Function Variants
Germline homozygous or compound heterozygous EFEMP2 variants. Most are missense substitutions in the calcium-binding EGF-like domains (p.Glu57Lys, p.Glu126Lys, p.Glu161Lys, p.Asp203Ala, p.Cys267Tyr, p.Arg279Cys, p.Ala397Thr); frameshift and nonsense alleles also occur, and a 4-bp duplication allele is degraded by nonsense-mediated decay. All act by loss of function, through absent transcript, impaired secretion, or lost binding to partners.
EFEMP2 hgnc:3219 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves EFEMP2 (hgnc:3219). hgnc:3219 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Genetic context variant_origin: GERMLINE zygosity: HOMOZYGOUS functional_impact_category: LOSS_OF_FUNCTION

Mutant Fibulin-4 Intracellular Retention and Reduced Secretion
Reduced Fibulin-4 in the Extracellular Matrix Direct route for null alleles removed by nonsense-mediated decay; missense alleles reach the same state through reduced secretion.
Defective Copper Loading of Lysyl Oxidase Intracellular role: fibulin-4 acts in the trans-Golgi network, before secretion, so this branch leaves the trigger directly rather than via the matrix deficit.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:16685658 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"We identified a patient with recessive inheritance of a missense mutation (169G-->A; E57K) in the Fibulin-4 gene."
First report of a recessive EFEMP2 (fibulin-4) allele, E57K.
PMID:17937443 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"fibulin-4 mRNAs with the 4-bp duplication transcribed from one allele are probably subject to nonsense-mediated decay, whereas synthesis and secretion of the missense R279C fibulin-4 protein from the other allele is severely impaired"
Two loss-of-function routes in one patient's fibroblasts: transcript decay for the frameshift allele and failed secretion for the missense allele.
PMID:27339457 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"different mutations in the fibulin-4 gene result in different molecular defects affecting secretion rates, protein stability, LOX-induced cross-linking, or binding to other ECM components"
Recombinant mutant proteins show allele-specific loss-of-function mechanisms.
Mutant Fibulin-4 Intracellular Retention and Reduced Secretion
Missense fibulin-4 (E57K, E126K, C267Y, R279C) is synthesized but secreted inefficiently; E57K is prone to dimer formation. Shown in patient fibroblasts, recombinant HEK293 expression and E57K knock-in fibroblasts.

Endoplasmic Reticulum Stress in Fibroblasts
An ER stress response to retained E57K fibulin-4 in dermal fibroblasts of knock-in mice. Whether it contributes to tissue disease beyond the loss of matrix fibulin-4 has not been tested, so no downstream edge is drawn.

PMID:26178373 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"thereby triggering an endoplasmic reticulum stress response"
Observed in cultured dermal fibroblasts from E57K mice.
Reduced Fibulin-4 in the Extracellular Matrix
Little or no fibulin-4 reaches the elastic fiber surface of skin, lung and aortic wall. This is the ECM defect of the aortic wall in the conserved aortopathy chain, with fibulin-4 substituting for the fibrillin-1 or collagen III lesions of other heritable aortopathies. The module node's process (elastic fiber assembly, decreased) is carried on the downstream Defective Elastic Fiber Assembly node rather than bundled here; from this node the mechanism forks to the lysyl oxidase, tropoelastin and LTBP-4 branches of elastogenesis, the smooth muscle maturation and elastic lamina to smooth muscle connection branches of the aortic wall, and to increased TGF-beta signalling.

Increased TGF-beta Signaling in the Aortic Wall The two Erasmus MC mouse lines disagree: reduced fibulin-4 (R/R) increases smooth muscle TGF-beta signalling, mainly through TGF-beta2, whereas complete smooth muscle absence of fibulin-4 does not. How the matrix deficit raises TGF-beta2 is not known.

Defective Copper Loading of Lysyl Oxidase
Fibulin-4 is needed for transfer of copper from ATP7A to LOX in the trans-Golgi network. Without it the lysine tyrosyl quinone (LTQ) cofactor does not form and secreted LOX is less active. Shown in Fbln4-null cells.

Mechanistic finding in Fbln4-null cells.
Impaired Proteolytic Maturation of Lysyl Oxidase
Processing of the LOX precursor to the mature enzyme is reduced in fibulin-4 deficient osteoblasts and in E57K knock-in skin.
Downstream
Reduced Lysyl Oxidase Activity
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:26178373 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Processing of a precursor to mature lysyl oxidase, an enzyme involved in cross-linking of elastin and collagen, was compromised."
Shown in skin of mice carrying the patient E57K allele.
PMID:26220971 REFUTE PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"the level of mature LOX was maintained to that of wild-type aortas"
In smooth muscle knockout aortas, mature LOX was not reduced, so this step may be tissue-specific.
Impaired Lysyl Oxidase Recruitment onto Tropoelastin
Fibulin-4 binds the LOX propeptide and forms ternary complexes with LOX and tropoelastin that position the enzyme for elastin cross-linking. The E57K patient allele strongly reduces binding to the LOX propeptide.

PMID:19855011 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"We also found a specific interaction between FBLN4 and the propeptide of LOX, which efficiently promotes assembly of LOX onto tropoelastin."
The binding that is lost when fibulin-4 is deficient.

A patient allele disrupts the interaction.
Reduced Lysyl Oxidase Activity
Convergence of the copper-loading and proteolytic-maturation defects: LOX made by fibulin-4 deficient cells has less catalytic activity. LOX cross-links both elastin and fibrillar collagen, so the mechanism forks here.

Deficient Elastin Cross-Linking
Mechanism confidence: Provisional
Reduced desmosine, the elastin-specific cross-link, in Fbln4-null tissues and in E57K knock-in skin. Arterial elastin cross-linking was normal in E57K mice and in smooth muscle knockout aortas, so this step is established for skin and the germline null but contested for the artery (see the discussion on elastin cross-linking).

Reduced Tropoelastin Expression

Fibulin-4 knockdown lowers tropoelastin transcription in human fibroblasts, and the aorta of knockdown or knockout mice has less tropoelastin. Another group found tropoelastin mRNA unchanged in Fbln4-null mice, so the step is contested.

Impaired Tropoelastin Deposition onto LTBP-4L
Fibulin-4 multimers switch LTBP-4L from a compact to an extended conformation and escort tropoelastin onto it; without fibulin-4 this LTBP-4L-dependent route of elastin deposition fails. In vitro evidence only.

Defective Elastic Fiber Assembly
Elastin fails to form intact cross-linked fibers. Patient skin has underdeveloped, sparse and fragmented elastic fibers. Assembly is systemic, so the mechanism branches by tissue: dermis, lung, conduit arteries and the diaphragm and abdominal wall.

Deficient Collagen Cross-Linking
Fewer lysyl oxidase-derived collagen cross-links in bone (lysylpyridinoline and hydroxylysylpyridinoline), aorta and E57K knock-in skin. This collagen arm, absent from FBLN5 disease, is thought to explain why EFEMP2 disease reaches collagen-rich tissues.

Downstream
Joint laxity Through poorly cross-linked, disorganised tendon and ligament collagen. Fibulin-4 null tendons assemble fewer, disordered collagen fibrils, and the same paper names joint laxity among the patients' connective tissue abnormalities.
Arachnodactyly Placed on the collagen arm because arachnodactyly travels with the skeletal features, but no mechanism has been established: nothing explains why fibulin-4 loss lengthens the digits, and the fibulin-4 null mouse has no equivalent finding.
Retrognathia Inferred placement on the collagen arm, since the mandible is a collagen-rich bone formed by the same cross-linking-dependent matrix assembly. No mechanism has been shown, in patients or in mice.
Hypertelorism Inferred, and weakly: orbital spacing depends on craniofacial skeletal growth, which the collagen cross-linking defect could plausibly disturb, but no study has connected the two in this disorder.
Flexion contractures
Multiple prenatal fractures Inferred from the bone collagen cross-link deficit. Fbln4-null mice did not show skeletal fragility, so the link rests on the human phenotype (see the mouse and human mismatch discussion).
Aortic Medial Degeneration
Diaphragm and Abdominal Wall Connective Tissue Weakness Inferred: collagen-rich supporting tissues are affected in EFEMP2 disease, but the diaphragm and inguinal canal have not been examined.

In Fibulin-4 mice the lungs have diminished antiprotease capacity, which worsens alveolar breakdown with age and infection in heterozygous (+/R) animals. An amplifier of emphysema, not its cause; mouse only.

Conduit Artery Elastic Lamina Fragmentation
Fragmented, moth-eaten elastic laminae with disorganized smooth muscle in the aorta and other large conducting arteries; resistance and muscular arteries are spared in E57K mice. In patients the aortic and central lung vessel media show elastic fiber fragmentation with cystic cavities.

Impaired Smooth Muscle Cell Contractile Maturation
Aortic smooth muscle cells of fibulin-4 deficient mice stay immature: lower contractile gene expression and smooth muscle myosin heavy chain, focal proliferation, ERK1/2 activation in the aneurysmal wall, and reduced aortic contractile force. Fibulin-4 is required for terminal smooth muscle differentiation as well as for elastic fibers.

Loss of Elastic Lamina-Smooth Muscle Cell Connections
The elastin-contractile units that attach smooth muscle cells to the elastic laminae fail to form in fibulin-4 smooth muscle knockout aortas, by postnatal day 7, before aneurysm. Deleting Fbln4 after these connections formed (at P7) prevents aneurysm, so the developmental connection defect is required.

PAR1 Activation in Aortic Smooth Muscle Cells Through abnormal mechanosensing: without the connections, stretch is transmitted abnormally to smooth muscle cells, which with thrombin and MMP-9 activate PAR1.
Ascending Aortic Dilation and Aneurysm
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26486174 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Postnatal deletion of Fbln4 in SMCs at P7 prevented cofilin activation and aneurysm formation, suggesting that these processes required disruption of elastic lamina-SMC connections."
Timing experiment shows the connection defect is needed for aneurysm.
PAR1 Activation in Aortic Smooth Muscle Cells
The mechanosensing arm of the aortic mechanism. In fibulin-4 smooth muscle knockout aortas, PAR1 protein and transcript rise by postnatal day 1, before any aneurysm, together with active thrombin and MMP-9, both PAR1 activators; cyclic stretch of smooth muscle cells induces PAR1 and prothrombin secretion, and thrombin then induces the mechanosensitive transcription factor Egr1 through PAR1. Egr1 in turn drives angiotensin-converting enzyme and local angiotensin II signalling and induces thrombospondin-1, which disrupts the elastin-contractile units further and, through slingshot-1 and cofilin, remodels the actin cytoskeleton. Deleting Egr1 or Thbs1 prevents most aneurysms, so this cascade is required rather than incidental in the mouse. PAR1, Egr1 and thrombin are also raised in human thoracic aortic aneurysm tissue, but not specifically in EFEMP2 patients.

Increased TGF-beta Signaling in the Aortic Wall
Mechanism confidence: Provisional
Raised TGF-beta pathway activity in the fibulin-4 deficient aortic media, the shared hub of the heritable aortopathies. In patients, pSmad2 and CTGF immunostaining of aortic and lung tissue is increased, and pSmad2 is raised on immunoblot of patient fibroblasts. In the hypomorphic mouse, aortic transcriptome and network analysis and phosphorylated Smad2 staining both implicate the pathway, tissue angiotensin II is raised, and isolated smooth muscle cells show increased Smad2 phosphorylation with markedly increased TGF-beta2 rather than TGF-beta1. Losartan lowers TGF-beta2. Whether the rise drives medial degeneration or merely marks the damaged matrix is unsettled: smooth muscle cells that lack fibulin-4 entirely do not show increased TGF-beta activation yet still form aneurysms.

Aortic Medial Degeneration
Cystic medial degeneration of the aortic wall, the histologic hallmark the disorder shares with the other heritable aortopathies: elastin fiber fragmentation, loss of smooth muscle cells, and deposition of ground substance in the matrix of the media. It is documented in the fibulin-4 hypomorphic mouse at both gene doses and in human EFEMP2 tissue, where autopsy shows elastic fiber fragmentation with cystic cavities in the aortic media and resected aneurysm specimens show medial thickening with torn elastic fibers. The degenerate media no longer carries pulsatile load, which is what makes the wall dilate.

Ascending Aortic Dilation and Aneurysm
Progressive dilation of the aortic root and ascending aorta, with elongation and tortuosity of the thoracic aorta and of the pulmonary arteries and their branches. This is the dominant clinical problem of the disorder and the feature that is present even when the skin is normal: in the Saudi p.Glu161Lys families the echocardiographic Z-score ranged from 5 to 33 with no cutis laxa at all. In the mouse the same lesion arises at every level of fibulin-4 reduction, and it progresses, so patients need lifelong imaging and often more than one operation.

UBERON:0001496 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Downstream
Aortic Dissection and Rupture The module's terminal edge. Rupture and dissection are documented in fibulin-4 null mice and in the hypomorph; in patients, death is more often from cardiopulmonary failure or from inoperable vascular disease than from a documented dissection.
Aortic aneurysm The clinical manifestation of this node, recorded as a phenotype so the disorder's dominant feature is queryable as such.
Arterial tortuosity
Emphysema Not a mechanistic edge in the matrix sense: a large ascending aneurysm can compress the airway and so worsen respiratory failure. Recorded as indirect with unknown intermediates because the compressive route has only case-level support.

Aortic Dissection and Rupture
The terminal vascular consequence. Fibulin-4 null mice die perinatally with arterial rupture and haemorrhage, and dissection of the aortic wall occurs in the hypomorph. In patients the corresponding endpoint is death from cardiopulmonary failure, inoperable systemic vascular disease, or bleeding from fragile vessels: 17 of 21 homozygotes in the South Indian founder cohort died at a median age of four months. A documented aortic dissection is reported less often in this disorder than in Marfan or Loeys-Dietz syndrome, partly because affected infants die before an aorta large enough to dissect develops, which is why surveillance and prophylactic replacement are the management mainstay.

Genetic Associations

1
EFEMP2
Gene: EFEMP2 hgnc:3219 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is EFEMP2 (hgnc:3219). hgnc:3219 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE variant_origin: GERMLINE
Show evidence (4 references)
CGGV:assertion_8911130f-a1fc-4404-8905-5632a49a95fe-2024-08-23T160000.000Z SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"In summary, EFEMP2 is definitively associated with autosomal recessive cutis laxa type 1B and aortic aneurysm."
ClinGen Definitive gene-disease validity for EFEMP2 and MONDO:0013754, classified by the Hereditary Cardiovascular Disease Gene Curation Expert Panel on 2024-08-23.
CGGV:assertion_8911130f-a1fc-4404-8905-5632a49a95fe-2024-08-23T160000.000Z SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The mechanism of pathogenicity is proposed to be loss of function."
The expert panel's mechanism call, consistent with the functional studies.
PMID:16685658 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"We identified a patient with recessive inheritance of a missense mutation (169G-->A; E57K) in the Fibulin-4 gene."
The original gene-disease report.
+ 1 more reference
Variants (7)
c.169G>A (p.Glu57Lys) Pathogenic
missense variant
The first reported and a recurrent allele, in the first calcium-binding EGF-like domain. The protein is made but dimerizes, is inefficiently secreted and triggers an endoplasmic reticulum stress response; it also binds the lysyl oxidase propeptide less well. The knock-in mouse carrying it survives to adulthood with loose skin, bent forelimb, aortic aneurysm, tortuous arteries and emphysema.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26178373 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Here we generated a knock-in mouse strain bearing a recurrent fibulin-4 E57K homozygous missense mutation."
Confirms E57K is a recurrent patient allele and names the model built from it.
c.608A>C (p.Asp203Ala) Pathogenic
missense variant
The South Indian founder allele, in a conserved calcium-binding EGF-like sequence in exon 7. Homozygous in 21 of 22 infants in the Kerala cohort and compound heterozygous with p.Arg227Cys in one. Homozygosity was lethal in 17 of 21, at a median age of four months.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:22943132 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Genetic studies revealed an identical c.608A > C (p. Asp203Ala) mutation in exon 7 of the FBLN4 gene in all 22 patients, homozygous in 21, and compound heterozygous in one patient with a p. Arg227Cys mutation in the same conserved cbEGF sequence."
Founder allele identification and zygosity in the largest cohort.
p.Glu161Lys Pathogenic
missense variant
The Saudi founder allele, homozygous in all nine affected subjects from four unrelated consanguineous families. This genotype presents as severe recessive aortic aneurysm with no skin manifestations, and two relatives were found only on family screening at 17 and 20 years of age.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:22440127 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Sequence analysis of EFEMP2, located on chromosome 11 at q13, identified a novel homozygous mutation (p.E161K) in all 9 affected subjects."
Founder allele shared across four consanguineous families.
c.835C>T (p.Arg279Cys) with c.1070_1073dupCCGC Pathogenic
missense variant
The compound heterozygous genotype of a newborn who died at 27 days of pulmonary artery occlusion. The duplication allele is degraded by nonsense-mediated decay and the missense allele is barely secreted, so the patient's fibroblasts deposited no fibulin-4 at all.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:17937443 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"DNA analysis identified compound heterozygous mutations ((c.835C > T (p.R279C)/c.1070_1073dupCCGC) in fibulin-4, a recently recognized elastic fiber associated protein."
The compound heterozygous genotype in a perinatally lethal case.
c.639C>A (p.Cys213Ter) Pathogenic
stop gained
Homozygous nonsense allele in two related fetuses whose pregnancies were terminated after second-trimester findings. Truncating genotypes sit at the lethal end of the severity range.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:30140196 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Sequencing of the EFEMP2 gene revealed a novel homozygous nonsense mutation: c.639C>A (p.Cys213*)."
Nonsense allele in the antenatally diagnosed severe cases.
p.Cys267Tyr Pathogenic
missense variant
Homozygous missense allele in a newborn of consanguineous parents with fetal overgrowth, contractural arachnodactyly, microcephaly and fatal soft tissue bleeding from fragile vessels. Impairs secretion of the protein in vitro.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:19664000 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Sequencing of the elastin, fibulin-4 and fibulin-5 genes revealed a homozygous missense mutation (p.Cys267Tyr) in the fibulin-4 gene in the patient."
Allele identification in the lethal overgrowth case.
p.Glu126Lys, p.Ala397Thr and p.Glu126Val with c.577delC Pathogenic
missense variant
Three genotypes found by screening 22 patients referred for arterial tortuosity, stenosis and aneurysms: two homozygous missense genotypes and one compound heterozygote with a frameshift. p.Glu126Lys loses binding to collagen IV, fibrillin-1, LTBP-1 and LTBP-4, and p.Ala397Thr loses LTBP-1 binding.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:20389311 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"two homozygous missense mutations (p.Glu126Lys and p.Ala397Thr) and compound heterozygosity for missense mutation p.Glu126Val and frameshift mutation c.577delC"
Three further pathogenic genotypes from a vascular referral cohort.

Medical Actions

10
Angiotensin Receptor Blockade for Aortic Root Dilatation
Action: angiotensin receptor blocker therapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is angiotensin receptor blocker therapy, annotated with Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Pharmacotherapy NCIT:C15986
Agent: losartan CHEBI:6541 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses losartan (CHEBI:6541). CHEBI:6541 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest. angiotensin II receptor antagonist NCIT:C66930 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses angiotensin II receptor antagonist (NCIT:C66930). NCIT:C66930 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Platform: Small molecule
Losartan or another angiotensin receptor blocker, offered on the strength of the mouse data and by analogy with Marfan syndrome. GeneReviews says it can be considered rather than recommending it. In the fibulin-4 smooth muscle knockout, losartan or captopril completely prevented aneurysm, but only inside a narrow window in the first month of life and without reversing the mechanical properties of the wall; in the adult hypomorph losartan reduced aneurysm size and improved survival without rescuing elastic fiber fragmentation. There is no human trial, and whether an equivalent early window exists in patients is unknown.
Mechanism Target:
Increased TGF-beta Signaling in the Aortic Wall
Ascending Aortic Dilation and Aneurysm
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:21563328 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Treatment of aortic root dilatation with beta-blockers or angiotensin receptor inhibitors can be considered."
The GeneReviews recommendation, stated permissively.
PMID:23636094 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"aneurysm formation was completely prevented by inhibition of the AngII pathway with losartan or captopril within a narrow therapeutic window during the first month of life"
The strongest preclinical result, with its timing caveat stated in the same sentence.
PMID:26828783 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"No drug rescued elastic fiber fragmentation in established aneurysms, although losartan did reduce aneurysm size."
Benefit in established disease is partial and does not restore wall structure.
Beta-Blockade for Aortic Root Dilatation
Action: beta-adrenergic blocker therapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is beta-adrenergic blocker therapy, annotated with Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Pharmacotherapy NCIT:C15986
Agent: beta-adrenergic antagonist NCIT:C29576 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses beta-adrenergic antagonist (NCIT:C29576). NCIT:C29576 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus. propranolol CHEBI:8499 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses propranolol (CHEBI:8499). CHEBI:8499 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Beta-blockers to reduce haemodynamic stress on the aortic wall, offered on the same permissive GeneReviews footing as angiotensin receptor blockade and used with it in reported patients. The preclinical evidence is negative: propranolol had no effect on survival, aneurysm growth or cardiac function in aneurysmal fibulin-4 mice, whereas losartan did.
Mechanism Target:
Ascending Aortic Dilation and Aneurysm
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:24276535 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"She was initially treated with an angiotensin II receptor blocker and beta-blocker and eventually underwent total thoracic aortic replacement"
Reported clinical use, as a bridge to surgery rather than a definitive therapy.
PMID:26828783 REFUTE PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Losartan, but not aliskiren or propranolol, improved survival in fibulin-4 mice."
Cuts against beta-blockade in this specific disorder: propranolol gave no survival benefit in the fibulin-4 hypomorph.
Aortic Aneurysm Replacement Surgery
Action: aortic aneurysm replacementNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is aortic aneurysm replacement, annotated with Surgical Procedure (NCIT:C15329). NCIT:C15329 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Surgical Procedure NCIT:C15329
Platform: Surgery
Open replacement of the aneurysmal segment, the only intervention with documented success. Reported operations range from ascending aortic replacement in a 30-month-old child to valve-sparing root and total arch replacement and two-stage elephant trunk procedures for total thoracic aortic replacement. Residual tortuosity and aneurysm risk persist in unreplaced segments, and redo surgery for conduit stenosis or new aneurysm is reported, so operation does not end surveillance.
Mechanism Target:
Ascending Aortic Dilation and Aneurysm
Aortic Dissection and Rupture
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:21563328 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Aortic aneurysm replacement has been performed successfully."
GeneReviews management statement on surgical replacement.
PMID:42404516 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"The aneurysm gradually enlarged in size, and the patient required ascending aortic replacement using the J-Graft with a diameter of 24 mm."
Successful replacement in a toddler, showing how early surgery may be needed.
PMID:36518920 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Re-do valve sparing root and arch replacement gained excellent restoration of the aorta; 1-year-follow-up was uneventful."
Reoperation is feasible, which matters because a first repair is often not the last.

Tracheostomy for Upper Airway Obstruction
Action: tracheostomyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is tracheostomy, annotated with Tracheotomy (NCIT:C15341). NCIT:C15341 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Tracheotomy NCIT:C15341
Platform: Surgery
Tracheostomy when retrognathia obstructs the upper airway. Airway compromise in this disorder has two separate causes, the retrognathic jaw and external compression of the airway by a large aneurysm, and only the former is an indication for tracheostomy.
Mechanism Target:
Retrognathia
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21563328 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Tracheostomy may be necessary when retrognathia leads to upper-airway obstruction."
The GeneReviews indication, tied specifically to retrognathia.

Cardiovascular and Pulmonary Surveillance
Action: cardiovascular and pulmonary surveillanceNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is cardiovascular and pulmonary surveillance, annotated with Supportive Care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Supportive Care NCIT:C15747
Platform: Other
Annual review by a cardiologist and a pulmonologist from diagnosis, with annual magnetic resonance angiography from head to pelvis, because the arteriopathy is systemic and progressive and new aneurysms arise in segments that were previously normal. Echocardiographic screening of siblings is part of the same programme and has found affected relatives in their late teens and twenties who had no symptoms.
Mechanism Target:
Ascending Aortic Dilation and Aneurysm
Aortic Dissection and Rupture
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:21563328 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Follow-up evaluations with a cardiologist and pulmonologist at least annually starting from the time of diagnosis. Annual MR angiography from head to pelvis."
The GeneReviews surveillance protocol.
PMID:22440127 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Intrafamilial variability was also evident; 2 unrelated subjects were detected at 17 and 20 years of age through family screening."
Family screening finds asymptomatic affected relatives with severe aneurysm.
Smoking and Sun-Tanning Avoidance
Action: smoking and sun-tanning avoidanceNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is smoking and sun-tanning avoidance, annotated with Smoking Cessation Intervention (NCIT:C15372). NCIT:C15372 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Smoking Cessation Intervention NCIT:C15372
Platform: Behavioral / lifestyle
Avoidance of cigarette smoking, to keep from worsening the emphysema, and of sun tanning, to avoid further damaging already deficient dermal elastic fibers. In the mouse, heterozygous animals acquire alveolar breakdown with age and on infectious stress and have reduced antiprotease capacity, which gives the smoking advice a mechanistic rationale.
Mechanism Target:
Reduced Pulmonary Antiprotease Capacity
Emphysema
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21563328 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Sun tanning to avoid damaging the skin; cigarette smoking to avoid worsening of emphysema."
The GeneReviews agents and circumstances to avoid.
Genetic Counseling and Reproductive Options
Action: genetic counselingNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is genetic counseling (NCIT:C15240). NCIT:C15240 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Genetic Counseling NCIT:C15240
Counseling on the 25% recurrence risk for each sib, carrier testing of at-risk relatives once the familial variants are known, and prenatal testing or preimplantation genetic testing. This matters especially in the consanguineous families and founder populations in which the disorder clusters.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21563328 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"carrier testing for at-risk relatives, prenatal testing for a pregnancy at increased risk, and preimplantation genetic testing are possible"
The GeneReviews genetic counseling options.
🔬

Diagnosis

6
EFEMP2 molecular genetic testing
The diagnosis rests on biallelic pathogenic EFEMP2 variants in a proband with suggestive findings. In practice the gene is reached through a multigene panel for cutis laxa or heritable thoracic aortic disease, or through exome or genome sequencing; targeted founder-variant testing is appropriate in the South Indian and Saudi populations. Exome sequencing has resolved cases misdiagnosed as Takayasu arteritis and has made the diagnosis prenatally. Chromosomal microarray, karyotype, mitochondrial and repeat-expansion testing have no role.
Genetic Testing NCIT:C15709 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:21563328 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"The diagnosis of EFEMP2-related cutis laxa is established in a proband with suggestive findings and biallelic pathogenic variants in EFEMP2 identified by molecular genetic testing."
The GeneReviews diagnostic statement.
PMID:38923535 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Prenatal trio exome sequencing identified two homozygous likely pathogenic variants, i.e. a missense in EFEMP2 involved in cutis laxa"
Exome sequencing can establish the diagnosis before birth.
Echocardiography
Echocardiography of the aortic root and ascending aorta, reported as a Z-score, is the first-line test and the instrument of family screening. Reported Z-scores in one founder cohort ran from 5 to 33.
Echocardiography Test NCIT:C16525 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:22440127 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"The index cases, their parents, and siblings underwent clinical evaluation and cardiac imaging."
Cardiac imaging as the screening instrument across whole families.
Computed tomography or magnetic resonance angiography
Whole-body angiography maps the distribution of dilatation, elongation and stenosis, which is what distinguishes this disorder from the other recessive cutis laxa types. In a 31-child imaging series, dilatation and elongation of the ascending aorta, arch, descending aorta and main pulmonary arteries with narrowing of the aortic isthmus were present in every patient, with stenoses at characteristic sites in roughly two thirds.
Computed Tomography Angiography NCIT:C202408 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:24838734 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Impressive dilatation and elongation of ascending aorta, arch, descending aorta and main pulmonary arteries with characteristic narrowing of aortic isthmus were seen in all patients."
The radiologic signature, present in every patient of a 31-child series.
PMID:24838734 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Stenosis of arch branches, abdominal visceral branches and pulmonary artery branches was observed in 21 (68%), 23 (62.5%) and 20 (65%) patients respectively."
Frequencies of stenosis by vascular territory on angiography.
Magnetic resonance angiography surveillance from head to pelvis
GeneReviews asks for annual magnetic resonance angiography covering head to pelvis, because new aneurysms and stenoses appear in territories that were previously normal and the whole arterial tree is at risk.
Magnetic Resonance Angiography NCIT:C190557 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21563328 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Annual MR angiography from head to pelvis."
The GeneReviews surveillance imaging interval and extent.
Skin biopsy for elastic fiber histology
Supportive rather than diagnostic: skin biopsy shows sparse, underdeveloped or fragmented elastic fibers, and research-grade staining of the patient's fibroblast matrix shows reduced or absent fibulin-4. A normal skin biopsy does not exclude the diagnosis, since the skin is spared in some founder genotypes.
Skin Biopsy NCIT:C51692 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:17937443 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Skin biopsy showed marked paucity and fragmentation of elastic fibers and autopsy revealed occlusion of the pulmonary artery."
The skin histology finding, alongside the vascular lesion.
PMID:16685658 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Her skin showed markedly underdeveloped elastic fibers, and the extracellular matrix laid down by her skin fibroblasts contained dramatically reduced amounts of fibulin-4."
Elastic fiber and fibulin-4 findings in skin and cultured fibroblasts.
Prenatal ultrasonography and fetal echocardiography
The cardinal features are detectable from the second trimester: dilated tortuous aorta, anomalies of the great vessels, retrognathia, clubfoot and fractures. Prenatal detection matters because the severe genotypes are lethal in infancy and because any therapeutic window suggested by the mouse work would fall in the first weeks of life.
Fetal Echocardiography Test NCIT:C114079 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:30140196 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Cardinal signs of this connective tissue disease were already seen during the second trimester of pregnancy, then confirmed and clarified at autopsy."
Second-trimester detectability of the phenotype.
PMID:38923535 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"ultrasonography findings including decreased fetal movements, hypoplastic male external genitalia, retrognathia, prefrontal edema, anomalies of the great vessels with pulmonary atresia and dilated tortuous aorta"
The prenatal ultrasound phenotype that prompted sequencing at 24 weeks.
📊

Prevalence

1
Worldwide
Cases/families reported in the literature Ultra-rare
Only three patients had been reported by 2009. The largest series since are 22 South Indian infants sharing p.Asp203Ala and nine Saudi patients from four families sharing p.Glu161Lys. No population prevalence estimate exists.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:19664000 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Our observation increases the number of cases with fibulin-4 mutations to three"
Literature case count as of 2009.
PMID:22943132 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Patients (11 males, 11 females) presented at median age of 1.5 months, belonging to unrelated families from identical ethno-geographical background"
Founder cluster in a single South Indian population subgroup.
PMID:22440127 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"we recruited 9 patients, from 4 unrelated consanguineous families, with recessively inherited AA"
Second founder cluster, in Saudi Arabia.
🐁

Animal Models

4
Fbln4 germline null mouse
Complete fibulin-4 loss abolishes elastogenesis. Null mice die perinatally with emphysema, arterial tortuosity, irregularity, aneurysm and rupture, develop no intact elastic fibers but irregular elastin aggregates, and have largely diminished elastin cross-links by desmosine analysis. The skeleton, in contrast, is morphologically normal apart from forelimb contractures and thick collagen fibrils.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
Fbln4-/- (gene-targeted null)
Publication
PMID:16478991
Pathograph links
Defective Elastic Fiber Assembly recapitulates fidelity: high observes: tissue
Limitations: A complete null, more severe than most patient missense genotypes, and perinatally lethal, so no postnatal course can be studied.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:16478991 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Histological analysis demonstrated that fibulin-4-/- mice do not develop intact elastic fibers but contain irregular elastin aggregates."
The elastic fiber assembly failure in its purest form.
Aortic Dissection and Rupture recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: tissue
Limitations: Murine rupture is perinatal and haemorrhagic, whereas the human endpoint is more often cardiopulmonary failure or inoperable vascular disease than a documented dissection.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:16478991 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"severe lung and vascular defects including emphysema, artery tortuosity, irregularity, aneurysm, rupture, and resulting hemorrhages"
Rupture and haemorrhage as the cause of perinatal death.
Fibulin-4 R/R hypomorphic mouse
A dosage series that survives into postnatal life. Homozygotes have ascending aortic dilatation, a tortuous stiffened aorta, thickened aortic valve leaflets with stenosis and insufficiency, cystic media degeneration, raised TGF-beta signalling and tissue angiotensin II, developmental lung emphysema, and death around three months. Heterozygotes acquire alveolar breakdown with age and infection and occasionally form small aneurysms. This is the line used for the losartan, aliskiren and propranolol comparisons.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
Fibulin-4(R/R) (four-fold reduced expression) and Fibulin-4(+/R) (two-fold)
Publication
PMID:17293478
Pathograph links
Aortic Medial Degeneration recapitulates fidelity: high observes: tissue
Limitations: The hypomorphic allele arose incidentally from targeting the adjacent Mus81 gene, so it models reduced fibulin-4 dose and not any patient allele.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21858106 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Medial degeneration is a key feature of aneurysm disease and aortic dissection."
The lesion this line was built to study, matched to human aneurysm pathology.
Increased TGF-beta Signaling in the Aortic Wall recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: cellular
Limitations: Smooth muscle cells with complete fibulin-4 loss do not show increased TGF-beta activation, so the dose matters and the finding does not generalise across genotypes.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26607280 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Significantly increased TGF-β2 levels were also detectable in plasma from homozygous Fibulin-4(R/R) mice, not in wild type mice."
The TGF-beta2 elevation is measurable in plasma, not only in tissue.
Fbln4 smooth muscle cell knockout mouse
The workhorse line for aortic mechanism and drug work. Deleting fibulin-4 in smooth muscle gives postnatal ascending aortic aneurysm with an immature contractile phenotype, focal proliferation, ERK1/2 activation, loss of the elastic lamina to smooth muscle connections by postnatal day 7, and then the PAR1, Egr1, ACE, angiotensin II, thrombospondin-1 and cofilin cascade. Deleting Egr1 or Thbs1, or deleting Fbln4 only after the connections have formed, prevents the aneurysm; losartan and captopril prevent it when given in the first month.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
Fbln4(SMKO) (SM22-Cre conditional deletion in smooth muscle)
Publication
PMID:20019329
Pathograph links
Impaired Smooth Muscle Cell Contractile Maturation recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: cellular
Limitations: Deletion is restricted to smooth muscle, so the model says nothing about skin, lung, diaphragm or bone, and it is a null allele rather than a patient missense allele.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:20019329 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"aortic walls fail to fully differentiate, exhibiting reduced expression of SM-specific contractile genes and focal proliferation of SMCs accompanied by degenerative changes of the medial wall"
Failed terminal differentiation in both germline and smooth muscle knockouts.
Loss of Elastic Lamina-Smooth Muscle Cell Connections recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: tissue Timing is the key result: deleting Fbln4 at postnatal day 7, after the connections are made, prevents aneurysm, which makes the developmental connection defect necessary rather than incidental.
Limitations: Conditional timing experiments of this kind have no human counterpart, so the necessity claim cannot be tested in patients.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26486174 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Postnatal deletion of Fbln4 in SMCs at P7 prevented cofilin activation and aneurysm formation, suggesting that these processes required disruption of elastic lamina-SMC connections."
The timing experiment establishing when the lesion must occur.
PAR1 Activation in Aortic Smooth Muscle Cells recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: cellular
Limitations: PAR1, Egr1 and thrombin are raised in human thoracic aortic aneurysm tissue, but that tissue was from unselected aneurysms rather than from EFEMP2 patients.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32580633 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Ingenuity Pathway Analysis identified PAR1 (protease-activated receptor 1) as a potential Egr1 upstream gene."
How PAR1 entered the cascade, as an unbiased pathway prediction later confirmed.
Deficient Elastin Cross-Linking fails to recapitulate fidelity: low observes: tissue Collagen cross-linking falls in knockout aortas, but elastin cross-linking is unaffected and mature lysyl oxidase is maintained, which is one half of the unresolved cross-linking conflict.
Limitations: Measured in aorta only; the same line's other tissues were not assayed, and the E57K skin does show reduced desmosine.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26220971 REFUTE PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"elastin cross-linking was unaffected and the level of mature LOX was maintained to that of wild-type aortas"
The aortic elastin cross-linking arm does not reproduce in this line. Quoted to the clause that carries this REFUTE claim: the earlier clauses of the same sentence report a cell-culture LOX measurement and a collagen result in aorta, both cited separately elsewhere, so quoting the whole sentence here graded one quote two ways.
Fbln4 E57K knock-in mouse
The only model of an actual patient allele. Homozygotes survive into adulthood with loose skin, bent forelimb, aortic aneurysm, arterial tortuosity, hypertension and pulmonary emphysema; the mutant protein is made but dimerizes, is poorly secreted and triggers ER stress; lysyl oxidase maturation is compromised, skin desmosine is reduced and dermal and tendon collagen fibrils are abnormal. The arterial lesion is confined to conduit vessels, with resistance and muscular arteries spared, and arterial elastin cross-linking and total elastin are unchanged.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
Fbln4(E57K/E57K) (knock-in of the recurrent human p.Glu57Lys allele)
Publication
PMID:26178373
Pathograph links
Mutant Fibulin-4 Intracellular Retention and Reduced Secretion recapitulates fidelity: high observes: cellular
Limitations: Faithful for p.Glu57Lys, but patient alleles fail by different routes, so a secretion-defect model does not represent the null or binding-defect alleles.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26178373 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"fibulin-4 E57K mutant protein was produced but was prone to dimer formation and inefficiently secreted, thereby triggering an endoplasmic reticulum stress response"
The allele's molecular defect, reproduced in the mouse.
Conduit Artery Elastic Lamina Fragmentation recapitulates fidelity: high observes: tissue
Limitations: Mouse blood pressure and aortic geometry differ from human, and these animals are hypertensive, which adds a load that most patients do not have.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28508064 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"We found a differential role of FBLN4 in elastic fiber assembly, where it functions mainly in large conduit arteries."
The vessel-type selectivity that this model established.
Deficient Elastin Cross-Linking fails to recapitulate fidelity: low observes: tissue Arterial elastin cross-linking and total elastin content are unchanged in this line even though elastic fiber architecture is abnormal and aneurysms form, which is the other half of the cross-linking conflict.
Limitations: Tissue-specific: the same animals' skin does have reduced desmosine, so the negative result is about the artery, not the whole animal.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28508064 REFUTE PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Elastin cross-linking and total elastin content were unchanged in large or small arteries, whereas elastic fiber architecture was abnormal in large vessels."
Architecture is deranged without any change in cross-link density.

Source YAML

click to show 
name: EFEMP2-Related Cutis Laxa
creation_date: "2026-11-10T23:31:26Z"
category: Mendelian
disease_term:
  preferred_term: EFEMP2-Related Cutis Laxa
  term:
    id: MONDO:0013754
    label: cutis laxa, autosomal recessive, type 1B
description: >-
  EFEMP2-related cutis laxa (autosomal recessive cutis laxa type 1B, ARCL1B) is
  a rare congenital connective tissue disorder caused by biallelic
  loss-of-function variants in EFEMP2, which encodes the short fibulin
  fibulin-4. Fibulin-4 binds tropoelastin and the propeptide of lysyl oxidase
  (LOX), is needed for copper loading and proteolytic maturation of LOX, and
  so supports both elastin and collagen cross-linking; in the aortic wall it
  is also needed for smooth muscle cell maturation and for the connections
  between elastic laminae and smooth muscle cells. The dominant clinical
  problem is a vasculopathy of the large elastic arteries: ascending aortic
  and aortic root aneurysm, elongation and tortuosity of the aorta, pulmonary
  arteries and their branches, and branch-vessel stenosis, with aortic
  isthmus hypoplasia in the most severe infants. Cutis laxa, emphysema,
  diaphragmatic and inguinal hernias, joint laxity, arachnodactyly,
  retrognathia and, in the severest cases, prenatal fractures are variable.
  Severity ranges from fetal or infantile death to isolated aortopathy found
  on family screening in adulthood. Mouse models show increased aortic
  TGF-beta signalling, an ACE and angiotensin II driven aneurysm pathway
  initiated by abnormal smooth muscle mechanosensing, and collagen fibril
  defects. Management is surveillance, angiotensin receptor or beta-blockade,
  and surgical replacement of the aneurysmal aorta.
synonyms:
- ARCL1B
- Cutis laxa, autosomal recessive, type 1B
- Cutis laxa, autosomal recessive, type IB
- Autosomal recessive cutis laxa type 1B
- FBLN4-related cutis laxa
- Fibulin-4 deficiency
parents:
- hereditary disease
- autosomal recessive cutis laxa type 1
notes: >-
  Named after the GeneReviews chapter title, EFEMP2-Related Cutis Laxa, which
  has the same scope as MONDO:0013754: EFEMP2 disease is recessive only, so the
  gene-related name covers exactly the disease the MONDO term binds. The MONDO
  label is kept in synonyms. Nomenclature of the type I letters differs between
  sources: OMIM and MONDO call the EFEMP2 form type 1B (OMIM 614437), whereas
  the 2013 Callewaert cohort paper called EFEMP2 disease type Ia and FBLN5
  disease type Ib. Cutis laxa is not obligatory; it was present in about half
  of a South Indian founder cohort and absent in a Saudi family series with a
  different founder allele, which present as recessive aortic aneurysm alone.
  The GeneReviews chapter is the phenotype baseline. Nearly all of the
  mechanism downstream of the elastic fiber defect comes from mouse models
  (germline null, Fibulin-4 R/R hypomorph, smooth muscle knockout and E57K
  knock-in); in patients only reduced matrix fibulin-4, abnormal elastic
  fibers, cystic medial degeneration and increased pSmad2 and CTGF staining
  have been shown directly. No conforms_to is declared to the
  emphysema_protease_antiprotease_imbalance module: emphysema in Fibulin-4 R/R
  mice is developmental, and reduced antiprotease capacity was reported only
  as an exacerbating factor, not the cause.
classifications:
  harrisons_chapter:
  - classification_value: GENETICS_ENVIRONMENT_DISEASE

      Monogenic autosomal recessive disorder caused by biallelic EFEMP2
      variants.
  - classification_value: DERMATOLOGY
    notes: >-
      Cutis laxa is the name-giving feature, although it is variable.

    which are consanguineous, or compound heterozygous. Heterozygous carriers
    are asymptomatic. Each sib of an affected individual has a 25% chance of
    being affected. Expressivity varies within families.

- name: Biallelic EFEMP2 Loss-of-Function Variants
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  role: trigger
  description: >-
    Germline homozygous or compound heterozygous EFEMP2 variants. Most are
    missense substitutions in the calcium-binding EGF-like domains (p.Glu57Lys,
    p.Glu126Lys, p.Glu161Lys, p.Asp203Ala, p.Cys267Tyr, p.Arg279Cys,
    p.Ala397Thr); frameshift and nonsense alleles also occur, and a 4-bp
    duplication allele is degraded by nonsense-mediated decay. All act by
    loss of function, through absent transcript, impaired secretion, or lost
    binding to partners.
  genetic_context:
    variant_origin: GERMLINE
    zygosity: HOMOZYGOUS
    functional_impact_category: LOSS_OF_FUNCTION

  - preferred_term: EFEMP2

      id: hgnc:3219
      label: EFEMP2

- name: Reduced Fibulin-4 in the Extracellular Matrix
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  conforms_to: "aortopathy_tgfbeta_dysregulation#Aortic Wall ECM or Contractile Apparatus Defect"
  description: >-
    Little or no fibulin-4 reaches the elastic fiber surface of skin, lung and
    aortic wall. This is the ECM defect of the aortic wall in the conserved
    aortopathy chain, with fibulin-4 substituting for the fibrillin-1 or
    collagen III lesions of other heritable aortopathies. The module node's
    process (elastic fiber assembly, decreased) is carried on the downstream
    Defective Elastic Fiber Assembly node rather than bundled here; from this
    node the mechanism forks to the lysyl oxidase, tropoelastin and LTBP-4
    branches of elastogenesis, the smooth muscle maturation and elastic
    lamina to smooth muscle connection branches of the aortic wall, and to
    increased TGF-beta signalling.
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: aortic smooth muscle cell
    term:
      id: CL:0000359
      label: vascular associated smooth muscle cell
  - preferred_term: dermal fibroblast
    term:
      id: CL:0002551
      label: fibroblast of dermis
  molecular_functions:
  - preferred_term: fibulin-4 as an elasticity-conferring matrix constituent
    modifier: DECREASED
    term:
      id: GO:0030023
      label: extracellular matrix constituent conferring elasticity

- name: Loss of Elastic Lamina-Smooth Muscle Cell Connections
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  description: >-
    The elastin-contractile units that attach smooth muscle cells to the
    elastic laminae fail to form in fibulin-4 smooth muscle knockout aortas,
    by postnatal day 7, before aneurysm. Deleting Fbln4 after these
    connections formed (at P7) prevents aneurysm, so the developmental
    connection defect is required.

- name: PAR1 Activation in Aortic Smooth Muscle Cells
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    The mechanosensing arm of the aortic mechanism. In fibulin-4 smooth muscle
    knockout aortas, PAR1 protein and transcript rise by postnatal day 1,
    before any aneurysm, together with active thrombin and MMP-9, both PAR1
    activators; cyclic stretch of smooth muscle cells induces PAR1 and
    prothrombin secretion, and thrombin then induces the mechanosensitive
    transcription factor Egr1 through PAR1. Egr1 in turn drives
    angiotensin-converting enzyme and local angiotensin II signalling and
    induces thrombospondin-1, which disrupts the elastin-contractile units
    further and, through slingshot-1 and cofilin, remodels the actin
    cytoskeleton. Deleting Egr1 or Thbs1 prevents most aneurysms, so this
    cascade is required rather than incidental in the mouse. PAR1, Egr1 and
    thrombin are also raised in human thoracic aortic aneurysm tissue, but
    not specifically in EFEMP2 patients.
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: aortic smooth muscle cell
    term:
      id: CL:0000359
      label: vascular associated smooth muscle cell
  locations:
  - preferred_term: ascending aorta
    term:
      id: UBERON:0001496
      label: ascending aorta
  genes:

      id: hgnc:3537
      label: F2R

      id: hgnc:3238

- name: Increased TGF-beta Signaling in the Aortic Wall
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  role: central_effector
  conforms_to: "aortopathy_tgfbeta_dysregulation#TGF-beta Signaling Dysregulation"
  description: >-
    Raised TGF-beta pathway activity in the fibulin-4 deficient aortic media,
    the shared hub of the heritable aortopathies. In patients, pSmad2 and CTGF
    immunostaining of aortic and lung tissue is increased, and pSmad2 is raised
    on immunoblot of patient fibroblasts. In the hypomorphic mouse, aortic
    transcriptome and network analysis and phosphorylated Smad2 staining both
    implicate the pathway, tissue angiotensin II is raised, and isolated
    smooth muscle cells show increased Smad2 phosphorylation with markedly
    increased TGF-beta2 rather than TGF-beta1. Losartan lowers TGF-beta2.
    Whether the rise drives medial degeneration or merely marks the damaged
    matrix is unsettled: smooth muscle cells that lack fibulin-4 entirely do
    not show increased TGF-beta activation yet still form aneurysms.

- name: Aortic Medial Degeneration
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  role: effector
  conforms_to: "aortopathy_tgfbeta_dysregulation#Aortic Medial Degeneration and Wall Weakening"
  description: >-
    Cystic medial degeneration of the aortic wall, the histologic hallmark the
    disorder shares with the other heritable aortopathies: elastin fiber
    fragmentation, loss of smooth muscle cells, and deposition of ground
    substance in the matrix of the media. It is documented in the fibulin-4
    hypomorphic mouse at both gene doses and in human EFEMP2 tissue, where
    autopsy shows elastic fiber fragmentation with cystic cavities in the
    aortic media and resected aneurysm specimens show medial thickening with
    torn elastic fibers. The degenerate media no longer carries pulsatile load,
    which is what makes the wall dilate.
  locations:
  - preferred_term: tunica media of the aorta

- name: Ascending Aortic Dilation and Aneurysm
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  role: effector
  conforms_to: "aortopathy_tgfbeta_dysregulation#Progressive Aortic Dilation and Aneurysm"
  description: >-
    Progressive dilation of the aortic root and ascending aorta, with
    elongation and tortuosity of the thoracic aorta and of the pulmonary
    arteries and their branches. This is the dominant clinical problem of the
    disorder and the feature that is present even when the skin is normal: in
    the Saudi p.Glu161Lys families the echocardiographic Z-score ranged from 5
    to 33 with no cutis laxa at all. In the mouse the same lesion arises at
    every level of fibulin-4 reduction, and it progresses, so patients need
    lifelong imaging and often more than one operation.

- name: Aortic Dissection and Rupture
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  role: consequence
  conforms_to: "aortopathy_tgfbeta_dysregulation#Aortic Dissection and Rupture"
  description: >-
    The terminal vascular consequence. Fibulin-4 null mice die perinatally
    with arterial rupture and haemorrhage, and dissection of the aortic wall
    occurs in the hypomorph. In patients the corresponding endpoint is death
    from cardiopulmonary failure, inoperable systemic vascular disease, or
    bleeding from fragile vessels: 17 of 21 homozygotes in the South Indian
    founder cohort died at a median age of four months. A documented aortic
    dissection is reported less often in this disorder than in Marfan or
    Loeys-Dietz syndrome, partly because affected infants die before an aorta
    large enough to dissect develops, which is why surveillance and
    prophylactic replacement are the management mainstay.
  locations:
  - preferred_term: aorta
    term:
      id: UBERON:0000947
      label: aorta
  evidence:

      vascular defects including emphysema, artery tortuosity, irregularity,
      aneurysm, rupture, and resulting hemorrhages
    explanation: Arterial rupture and haemorrhage in the complete null.
  - reference: PMID:22943132
    reference_title: "Characterization of a distinct lethal arteriopathy syndrome in twenty-two infants associated with an identical, novel mutation in FBLN4 gene, confirms fibulin-4 as a critical determinant of human vascular elastogenesis."

- name: Cutis laxa
  category: Dermatologic
  description: >-
    Loose, redundant, inelastic skin, congenital and generalized when present.
    It names the disorder but is not obligate: 52% of the 22-infant South
    Indian founder cohort had it, and none of the nine Saudi p.Glu161Lys
    patients did, so EFEMP2 disease can present as recessive aortopathy with
    normal skin.

- name: Arterial tortuosity
  category: Cardiovascular
  description: >-

- name: Arterial stenosis
