- name: Aortic aneurysm
  category: Cardiovascular
  description: >-
    Aneurysmal dilatation of the aorta, the dominant clinical problem of the
    disorder and the feature present even when the skin is normal. The aortic
    root and ascending aorta are affected most, but the arch and descending
    aorta dilate and elongate too. Echocardiographic Z-scores ranged from 5 to
    33 across nine homozygous patients in the Saudi founder families, and the
    aneurysm progresses, so surveillance is lifelong and surgical replacement
    of one segment does not end the risk in the others.

genetic:
- name: EFEMP2
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: EFEMP2
    term:
      id: hgnc:3219
      label: EFEMP2
  relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
  variant_origin: GERMLINE
  presence: PRESENT
  features: >-
    EFEMP2, at 11q13, encodes fibulin-4 (also called FBLN4 and MBP1), a short
    fibulin built from tandem calcium-binding EGF-like domains and a
    C-terminal fibulin domain. Fibulin-4 binds tropoelastin, the lysyl oxidase
    propeptide, LTBP-1 and LTBP-4, fibrillin-1, collagen IV and the
    procollagen C-endopeptidase enhancer Pcolce, and it transfers copper from
    ATP7A to lysyl oxidase in the trans-Golgi network. Biallelic
    loss-of-function variants cause this disorder; most are missense
    substitutions in the calcium-binding EGF-like domains, and the molecular
    route to loss of function is allele-specific, running through transcript
    decay, impaired secretion, protein instability, or lost binding to
    individual partners. ClinGen's Hereditary Cardiovascular Disease expert
    panel classifies the gene-disease relationship as Definitive for autosomal
    recessive inheritance.
  notes: >-
    A heterozygous p.Arg83Cys allele has been proposed for dominant
    nonsyndromic thoracic aortic aneurysm on in-silico grounds alone
    (PMID:38113391); it is not reflected here because ClinGen's Definitive
    call covers autosomal recessive inheritance only and GeneReviews states
    that carriers are asymptomatic. In the mouse, a modest heterozygous dose
    reduction occasionally produced small aneurysms, so a weak heterozygous
    effect cannot be excluded on current evidence.
  evidence:

treatments:
- name: Angiotensin Receptor Blockade for Aortic Root Dilatation
  description: >-
    Losartan or another angiotensin receptor blocker, offered on the strength
    of the mouse data and by analogy with Marfan syndrome. GeneReviews says it
    can be considered rather than recommending it. In the fibulin-4 smooth
    muscle knockout, losartan or captopril completely prevented aneurysm, but
    only inside a narrow window in the first month of life and without
    reversing the mechanical properties of the wall; in the adult hypomorph
    losartan reduced aneurysm size and improved survival without rescuing
    elastic fiber fragmentation. There is no human trial, and whether an
    equivalent early window exists in patients is unknown.
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  treatment_term:

- name: Aortic Aneurysm Replacement Surgery
  description: >-
    Open replacement of the aneurysmal segment, the only intervention with
    documented success. Reported operations range from ascending aortic
    replacement in a 30-month-old child to valve-sparing root and total arch
    replacement and two-stage elephant trunk procedures for total thoracic
    aortic replacement. Residual tortuosity and aneurysm risk persist in
    unreplaced segments, and redo surgery for conduit stenosis or new aneurysm
    is reported, so operation does not end surveillance.

discussions:
- discussion_id: controversy_efemp2_tgfbeta_driver_or_consequence
  prompt: >-
    Is increased TGF-beta signalling in the fibulin-4 deficient aorta a driver
    of medial degeneration and aneurysm, or a consequence of the disordered
    matrix that marks damage without causing it?
  kind: CONTROVERSY
  status: OPEN
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#Increased TGF-beta Signaling in the Aortic Wall
  - pathophysiology#Aortic Medial Degeneration
  - treatments#Angiotensin Receptor Blockade for Aortic Root Dilatation
  rationale: >-
    The rise is well documented on both sides of the species divide: pSmad2
    and CTGF are increased in patient aortic and lung tissue, and the mouse
    transcriptome, Smad2 phosphorylation and TGF-beta2 levels all point the
    same way. What has never been shown is that blocking TGF-beta prevents the
    aneurysm. The strongest argument against a required role is that smooth
    muscle cells lacking fibulin-4 entirely do not raise TGF-beta activation,
    yet that genotype still produces thoracic aortic aneurysm, so the pathway
    cannot be the only route. The practical stake is that angiotensin receptor
    blockade is offered partly on a TGF-beta rationale, and losartan's benefit
    in the mouse is partial, early-window dependent and does not restore wall
    structure. Pending resolution, the conforms_to edge to the module's
    TGF-beta hub is declared with mechanism_confidence PROVISIONAL.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20389311
    reference_title: Altered TGFbeta signaling and cardiovascular manifestations in patients with autosomal recessive cutis laxa type I caused by fibulin-4 deficiency.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      we provide the first evidence for the involvement of altered TGFbeta
      signaling in the pathogenesis of FBLN4 mutations in humans
    explanation: >-
      The authors claim pathogenic involvement, but their data are
      immunostaining and immunoblotting, which show association rather than
      causation.
  - reference: PMID:30844428
    reference_title: Fibulin-4 deficiency differentially affects cytoskeleton structure and dynamics as well as TGFβ signaling.
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      Since both mouse models show thoracic aortic aneurysm formation, we
      conclude that not only hampered TGFβ signaling, but also SMA cytoskeleton
      dynamics play an important role in aortic aneurysmal disease.
    explanation: >-
      The authors' own conclusion that TGF-beta dysregulation is not sufficient
      to explain aneurysm formation in fibulin-4 deficiency.
  proposed_experiments:
  - experiment_id: exp_efemp2_tgfbeta_blockade_timed
    name: Timed TGF-beta neutralisation in fibulin-4 deficient mouse aorta
    description: >-
      Neutralise TGF-beta, or delete Smad2 or the type II receptor in smooth
      muscle cells, in both the Fbln4 smooth muscle knockout and the
      Fibulin-4 hypomorph, starting inside and outside the first postnatal
      month, and score aneurysm incidence, medial histology and wall mechanics
      against losartan as a positive control. The two genotypes differ in
      whether TGF-beta activation rises at all, so running both separates a
      required pathway from a marker of matrix damage.
- discussion_id: gap_efemp2_mouse_human_mismatch
  prompt: >-
    How far do the fibulin-4 mouse models represent the human disease,
    particularly for the skeletal phenotype, where patients have prenatal
    fractures but null mice have an essentially normal skeleton?
  kind: HUMAN_MODEL_MISMATCH
  status: OPEN
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#Deficient Collagen Cross-Linking
  - pathophysiology#Abnormal Collagen Fibril Assembly
  - pathophysiology#PAR1 Activation in Aortic Smooth Muscle Cells
  - phenotypes#Multiple prenatal fractures
  rationale: >-
    Almost everything in this entry downstream of the elastic fiber defect
    rests on mouse evidence; in patients only reduced matrix fibulin-4,
    abnormal elastic fibers, cystic medial degeneration and increased pSmad2
    and CTGF staining have been shown directly. The clearest mismatch is bone.
    Human truncating and founder genotypes produce innumerable prenatal
    fractures severe enough to be read as osteogenesis imperfecta type IIC,
    whereas fibulin-4 null mice have no skeletal morphological abnormality
    beyond forelimb contractures and thick collagen fibrils, despite the same
    biochemical lesion of reduced lysyl oxidase processing and collagen
    cross-linking. The mechanosensing arm has a second kind of gap: the
    PAR1-Egr1-ACE-Thbs1 cascade is established in mouse aorta and the proteins
    are raised in human thoracic aortic aneurysm tissue, but that human tissue
    was not from EFEMP2 patients. Separately, the main hypomorphic line arose
    from targeting the neighbouring Mus81 gene, so it is a dosage model and
    not a patient allele; the E57K knock-in is the only model of a human
    variant.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:26690653
    reference_title: "Loss of fibulin-4 results in abnormal collagen fibril assembly in bone, caused by impaired lysyl oxidase processing and collagen cross-linking."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      No morphological abnormalities were found in the skeleton of Fbln4(-/-)
      mice as compared to wild type littermates except forelimb contractures
      as well as unusually thick collagen fibrils.
    explanation: >-
      The mouse skeleton is spared the fractures that dominate severe human
      cases, although the underlying cross-linking lesion is present.
  - reference: PMID:22070778

  - reference: PMID:17293478

  proposed_experiments:
  - experiment_id: exp_efemp2_skeletal_phenotyping_knockin
    name: Skeletal phenotyping of patient-allele knock-in mice against human bone
    description: >-
      Phenotype the bone of E57K knock-in mice and of new knock-ins carrying
      the lethal human alleles p.Asp203Ala and p.Cys213Ter with microCT,
      biomechanical testing and collagen cross-link analysis, and compare
      against bone or skin fibroblasts from patients with the matching
      genotypes. This tests whether the absent murine fracture phenotype
      reflects a species difference in skeletal loading and collagen
      cross-link chemistry or simply an allele difference.
- discussion_id: gap_efemp2_elastin_crosslink_conflict
  prompt: >-
    Does fibulin-4 loss actually reduce elastin cross-linking in the artery
    wall? Null mouse tissue has largely diminished elastin cross-links, but
    the E57K knock-in and the smooth muscle knockout both have normal arterial
    elastin cross-linking while still developing aneurysm.
  kind: KNOWLEDGE_GAP
  status: OPEN
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#Deficient Elastin Cross-Linking
  - pathophysiology#Reduced Lysyl Oxidase Activity
  - pathophysiology#Impaired Lysyl Oxidase Recruitment onto Tropoelastin
  - pathophysiology#Conduit Artery Elastic Lamina Fragmentation
  rationale: >-
    The lysyl oxidase tethering model predicts that reduced fibulin-4 lowers
    elastin cross-linking everywhere it acts. Desmosine analysis of complete
    null tissue agrees. Two later models disagree for the artery: in the E57K
    knock-in, elastin cross-linking and total elastin content were unchanged
    in large and small arteries even though elastic fiber architecture was
    abnormal and aneurysms formed, and the authors say in as many words that
    this calls the tethering role into question; in the smooth muscle
    knockout, collagen cross-linking fell but elastin cross-linking was
    unaffected and mature lysyl oxidase was maintained. The results are not
    strictly incompatible, since they differ in allele, severity, tissue and
    assay, and the E57K skin does have reduced desmosine while its arteries do
    not. But the published positions cannot all be true of the aorta at once,
    and the resolution decides whether the aortic phenotype is a cross-linking
    disease at all or an elastic fiber architecture and mechanosensing disease.
    This entry therefore keeps the cross-linking arm pointed at skin, lung and
    bone and routes the aortic chain through the lamina-smooth muscle
    connection and mechanosensing nodes instead.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:16478991
    reference_title: Targeted disruption of fibulin-4 abolishes elastogenesis and causes perinatal lethality in mice.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      Desmosine analysis indicated that elastin cross-links in fibulin-4-/-
      tissues were largely diminished.
    explanation: The founding observation for reduced elastin cross-linking, in complete null tissue.
  - reference: PMID:28508064
    reference_title: Fibulin-4 is essential for maintaining arterial wall integrity in conduit but not muscular arteries.
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      Normal levels of elastin cross-links in mutant tissue call into question
      FBLN4's suggested role in mediating lysyl oxidase-elastin interactions.
    explanation: >-
      The authors explicitly challenge the tethering model on the basis of
      normal arterial elastin cross-links in a patient-allele knock-in.
  - reference: PMID:26220971
    reference_title: "Loss of fibulin-4 disrupts collagen synthesis and maturation: implications for pathology resulting from EFEMP2 mutations."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      collagen cross-linking was diminished in SMKO aortas; however, elastin
      cross-linking was unaffected and the level of mature LOX was maintained
      to that of wild-type aortas
    explanation: >-
      Dissociates the collagen and elastin arms in the aorta, with mature lysyl
      oxidase preserved.
  - reference: PMID:26178373
    reference_title: "Fibulin-4 E57K Knock-in Mice Recapitulate Cutaneous, Vascular and Skeletal Defects of Recessive Cutis Laxa 1B with both Elastic Fiber and Collagen Fibril Abnormalities."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      The knock-in skin had a reduced level of desmosine, an elastin-specific
      cross-link compound, and ultrastructurally abnormal elastic fibers.
    explanation: >-
      In the same mouse line whose arteries have normal cross-links, the skin
      does have reduced desmosine, which suggests the conflict is
      tissue-specific rather than a straight contradiction.
  proposed_experiments:
  - experiment_id: exp_efemp2_crosslink_assay_harmonisation
    name: Harmonised desmosine and collagen cross-link quantification across fibulin-4 alleles and tissues
    description: >-
      Measure desmosine, isodesmosine and lysyl pyridinoline cross-links by the
      same mass spectrometry protocol, in aorta, skin, lung, tendon and bone,
      across the null, hypomorph, smooth muscle knockout and E57K knock-in at
      matched ages, and in patient skin and aortic tissue where available.
      Report absolute cross-link density per unit elastin and collagen rather
      than relative change, so that allele, tissue and assay are no longer
      confounded.
- discussion_id: open_question_efemp2_genotype_phenotype
  prompt: >-
    What predicts severity in EFEMP2 disease? Truncating alleles and the
    p.Asp203Ala founder genotype are usually lethal in infancy while
    p.Glu161Lys homozygotes reach adulthood, but no formal correlation study
    exists and expressivity varies within single families.
  kind: OPEN_QUESTION
  status: OPEN
  attaches_to:
  - genetic#EFEMP2
  - phenotypes#Cutis laxa
  - phenotypes#Aortic isthmus hypoplasia
  rationale: >-
    Cohort-level patterns are suggestive. Truncating genotypes appear in fetal
    and perinatally lethal cases; p.Asp203Ala homozygosity killed 17 of 21
    infants at a median age of four months; p.Glu161Lys homozygosity gives
    severe aortic aneurysm with normal skin and survivors found incidentally
    at 17 and 20 years. Against a simple allele-severity rule, intrafamilial
    variability was explicit in the Saudi families, where relatives sharing
    one homozygous genotype ranged from cyanosis at three months to an
    asymptomatic adult, and the functional work shows that different missense
    alleles fail in different ways, losing secretion, stability, lysyl oxidase
    dependent cross-linking, or binding to individual partners, which is not a
    single severity axis. The one feature with reported prognostic weight is
    anatomical rather than molecular: isthmic hypoplasia correlated with death
    at or before four months. Because the alleles are founder alleles,
    population background and modifier genes are confounded with genotype in
    every cohort published so far.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:22440127
    reference_title: Recessively inherited severe aortic aneurysm caused by mutated EFEMP2.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      In the affected subjects, the clinical presentation ranged from sweating
      and cyanosis at 3 months of age to incidental findings in an
      asymptomatic adult.
    explanation: >-
      One homozygous genotype spans most of the clinical range, which limits
      how much genotype alone can predict.
  - reference: PMID:27339457
    reference_title: Functional consequence of fibulin-4 missense mutations associated with vascular and skeletal abnormalities and cutis laxa.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      The substitutions E126K and C267Y impaired secretion of the protein, but
      not mRNA synthesis.
    explanation: >-
      Missense alleles fail by different molecular routes, so severity is
      unlikely to follow a single ordering.
  proposed_experiments:
  - experiment_id: exp_efemp2_genotype_phenotype_registry
    name: Multi-centre EFEMP2 genotype-phenotype registry with standardised imaging
    description: >-
      Pool reported and unreported EFEMP2 families into one registry recording
      genotype, aortic root and ascending aorta Z-scores at matched ages,
      presence of isthmic hypoplasia, skin involvement, fractures and age and
      cause of death, then test allele class, residual secreted fibulin-4 in
      patient fibroblasts, and isthmic hypoplasia as competing predictors of
      survival. Including within-family pairs is essential, since intrafamilial
      variability is the main evidence against a genotype-only model.
- discussion_id: open_question_efemp2_aneurysm_management
  prompt: >-
    How should the aortopathy be managed? No human trial exists, the mouse
    evidence favours angiotensin blockade over beta-blockade and only inside
    an early window, and neither drug restores the aortic wall, so when and in
    whom to operate is unsettled.
  kind: OPEN_QUESTION
  status: OPEN
  attaches_to:
  - treatments#Angiotensin Receptor Blockade for Aortic Root Dilatation
  - treatments#Beta-Blockade for Aortic Root Dilatation
  - treatments#Aortic Aneurysm Replacement Surgery
  - treatments#Cardiovascular and Pulmonary Surveillance
  rationale: >-
    GeneReviews states only that beta-blockers or angiotensin receptor
    inhibitors can be considered, which is as far as the human evidence
    reaches. The preclinical data are sharper and less comfortable: losartan
    or captopril prevented aneurysm completely in the fibulin-4 smooth muscle
    knockout but only when given during the first postnatal month, and without
    reversing the altered mechanics of the wall; in adult hypomorphs losartan
    reduced aneurysm size and improved survival while aliskiren and
    propranolol did neither, and no drug rescued elastic fiber fragmentation
    in established aneurysms. If a comparable critical window exists in
    patients it would fall in infancy, before most patients are diagnosed, and
    in the founder cohort median presentation was at 1.5 months with most
    deaths by four months. Surgical thresholds are borrowed from other
    aortopathies, operations are reported from 19 months of age onward, and
    reoperation for conduit stenosis or a new aneurysm is reported, so neither
    the medical nor the surgical timing question has a disorder-specific
    answer.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:21563328
    reference_title: EFEMP2-Related Cutis Laxa.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      Treatment of aortic root dilatation with beta-blockers or angiotensin
      receptor inhibitors can be considered.
    explanation: >-
      The authoritative human recommendation is permissive, not directive,
      which is itself the measure of the evidence gap.
  - reference: PMID:23636094
    reference_title: Angiotensin-converting enzyme-induced activation of local angiotensin signaling is required for ascending aortic aneurysms in fibulin-4-deficient mice.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      even though the altered mechanical properties of blood vessel walls were
      not reversed by the pharmacological treatment
    explanation: >-
      Prevention of aneurysm without repair of the wall, which is why drug
      treatment does not remove the need for surveillance or surgery.
  - reference: PMID:26828783
    reference_title: "AT1-receptor blockade, but not renin inhibition, reduces aneurysm growth and cardiac failure in fibulin-4 mice."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      Although losartan and aliskiren reduced hemodynamic stress and increased
      renin similarly, only losartan increased survival.
    explanation: >-
      The benefit is not explained by load reduction alone, so drug choice
      within the class matters and cannot be inferred from blood pressure.
  proposed_experiments:
  - experiment_id: exp_efemp2_early_arb_trial
    name: Prospective early-initiation angiotensin receptor blocker cohort with imaging endpoints
    description: >-
      In founder populations where the genotype can be identified at or before
      birth by cascade or prenatal testing, start an angiotensin receptor
      blocker in the neonatal period in consenting families and compare aortic
      root and ascending aorta Z-score trajectories, isthmic anatomy and
      survival against genotype-matched historical and concurrent controls
      started later. A randomised trial is unlikely to be feasible at this
      prevalence, so a prospective genotype-matched cohort with prespecified
      imaging endpoints is the realistic design.
- discussion_id: gap_efemp2_unexplained_features
  prompt: >-
    Several reported features have no proposed mechanism in this entry: why
    some arterial segments narrow while others dilate, why the aortic isthmus
    is specifically hypoplastic, and how corneal thinning, baroreflex failure
    with cerebral vasospasm, fetal overgrowth and microcephaly arise from
    fibulin-4 loss.
  kind: KNOWLEDGE_GAP
  status: OPEN
  attaches_to:
  - phenotypes#Arterial stenosis
  - phenotypes#Aortic isthmus hypoplasia
  - pathophysiology#Conduit Artery Elastic Lamina Fragmentation
  rationale: >-
    The entry routes stenosis and isthmic hypoplasia from the conduit artery
    lamina defect with indirect edges and unknown intermediates, which is
    honest but uninformative: a single elastic fiber lesion is being asked to
    explain both dilation and narrowing in the same aorta, and the earliest
    abnormality in null embryos is narrowing of the descending aorta rather
    than dilation anywhere. Isthmic hypoplasia carries real prognostic weight,
    so its developmental basis is worth resolving rather than recording.
    Separately, single-case features are not modelled here at all because one
    report each supports them and no mechanism has been proposed: keratoglobus
    with corneal rupture in a 38-year-old, recurrent thunderclap headache with
    baroreflex failure and cerebral vasospasm in a child, and fetal overgrowth
    with microcephaly in the lethal p.Cys267Tyr case. Baroreflex failure is
    the most mechanistically suggestive of these, since baroreceptors sit in
    the elastic wall of the carotid sinus and aortic arch and would be
    expected to misreport strain in a disorganised elastic lamina, but that
    link has been proposed rather than tested.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:16478991
    reference_title: Targeted disruption of fibulin-4 abolishes elastogenesis and causes perinatal lethality in mice.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      The earliest abnormality noted was a uniformly narrowing of the
      descending aorta in fibulin-4-/- embryos at embryonic day 12.5 (E12.5).
    explanation: >-
      Narrowing precedes tortuosity and aneurysm in development, so stenosis
      is not simply a late remodelling response to dilation elsewhere.
  - reference: PMID:24733866
    reference_title: "Baroreflex failure, sympathetic storm, and cerebral vasospasm in fibulin-4 cutis laxa."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      we present the first case of a child with recurrent thunderclap headache
      and cerebral vasospasm with baroreflex failure secondary to vascular
      complications of a rare genetic connective tissue disorder
    explanation: >-
      A single case attributing baroreflex failure to the arteriopathy, with
      no mechanistic test of the attribution.
  - reference: PMID:31194159
    reference_title: Keratoglobus with ARCL1B (EFEMP2 gene) cutis laxa.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: >-
      We believe that this is the first reported case of keratoglobus
      associated with cutis laxa.
    explanation: >-
      Corneal thinning in an adult EFEMP2 patient, reported once and not
      otherwise explained.
  proposed_experiments:
  - experiment_id: exp_efemp2_segmental_aorta_development
    name: Segmental developmental mapping of the fibulin-4 deficient aorta
    description: >-
      Follow the fibulin-4 null and E57K embryonic aorta from E11.5 through
      birth with light-sheet imaging and segment-resolved single-cell
      transcriptomics, separating neural crest derived from somite derived and
      second heart field derived smooth muscle along the arch and isthmus, and
      ask whether narrowing segments differ from dilating segments in lineage,
      elastic lamina number, or local haemodynamic load. The same sections
      should be scored for baroreceptor afferent innervation of the carotid
      sinus and arch to test whether the baroreflex phenotype has a structural
      correlate.
diagnosis:
- name: EFEMP2 molecular genetic testing
  description: >-
    The diagnosis rests on biallelic pathogenic EFEMP2 variants in a proband
    with suggestive findings. In practice the gene is reached through a
    multigene panel for cutis laxa or heritable thoracic aortic disease, or
    through exome or genome sequencing; targeted founder-variant testing is
    appropriate in the South Indian and Saudi populations. Exome sequencing
    has resolved cases misdiagnosed as Takayasu arteritis and has made the
    diagnosis prenatally. Chromosomal microarray, karyotype, mitochondrial
    and repeat-expansion testing have no role.
  diagnosis_term:
    preferred_term: Genetic Testing
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15709
      label: Genetic Testing

  - reference: PMID:21563328
    reference_title: EFEMP2-Related Cutis Laxa.

    snippet: >-
      The diagnosis of EFEMP2-related cutis laxa is established in a proband
      with suggestive findings and biallelic pathogenic variants in EFEMP2
      identified by molecular genetic testing.
    explanation: The GeneReviews diagnostic statement.
  - reference: PMID:38923535
    reference_title: "Discovery of pathogenic variants in EFEMP2 and RAG1 and undetectable fetal phenotype: A challenge of prenatal exome sequencing."
    supports: SUPPORT

- name: Computed tomography or magnetic resonance angiography
  description: >-
    Whole-body angiography maps the distribution of dilatation, elongation and
    stenosis, which is what distinguishes this disorder from the other
    recessive cutis laxa types. In a 31-child imaging series, dilatation and
    elongation of the ascending aorta, arch, descending aorta and main
    pulmonary arteries with narrowing of the aortic isthmus were present in
    every patient, with stenoses at characteristic sites in roughly two thirds.

animal_models:
- name: Fbln4 germline null mouse
  species: Mouse
  genotype: Fbln4-/- (gene-targeted null)
  publication: PMID:16478991
  description: >-
    Complete fibulin-4 loss abolishes elastogenesis. Null mice die
    perinatally with emphysema, arterial tortuosity, irregularity, aneurysm
    and rupture, develop no intact elastic fibers but irregular elastin
    aggregates, and have largely diminished elastin cross-links by desmosine
    analysis. The skeleton, in contrast, is morphologically normal apart from
    forelimb contractures and thick collagen fibrils.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Defective Elastic Fiber Assembly
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: HIGH
    model_scale: TISSUE
    limitations: >-
      A complete null, more severe than most patient missense genotypes, and
      perinatally lethal, so no postnatal course can be studied.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:16478991
      reference_title: Targeted disruption of fibulin-4 abolishes elastogenesis and causes perinatal lethality in mice.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      snippet: >-
        Histological analysis demonstrated that fibulin-4-/- mice do not develop
        intact elastic fibers but contain irregular elastin aggregates.
      explanation: The elastic fiber assembly failure in its purest form.
  - target: Aortic Dissection and Rupture
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: TISSUE
    limitations: >-
      Murine rupture is perinatal and haemorrhagic, whereas the human endpoint
      is more often cardiopulmonary failure or inoperable vascular disease than
      a documented dissection.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:16478991
      reference_title: Targeted disruption of fibulin-4 abolishes elastogenesis and causes perinatal lethality in mice.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      snippet: >-
        severe lung and vascular defects including emphysema, artery
        tortuosity, irregularity, aneurysm, rupture, and resulting hemorrhages
      explanation: Rupture and haemorrhage as the cause of perinatal death.
  - target: Multiple prenatal fractures
    relationship: FAILS_TO_RECAPITULATE
    fidelity: LOW
    model_scale: ORGANISM
    description: >-
      Despite the same biochemical lesion of reduced lysyl oxidase processing
      and reduced bone collagen cross-links, the null skeleton has no
      morphological abnormality and no fractures.
    limitations: >-
      The clearest model-human mismatch in this disorder, recorded in the
      discussion on mouse-human differences.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:26690653
      reference_title: "Loss of fibulin-4 results in abnormal collagen fibril assembly in bone, caused by impaired lysyl oxidase processing and collagen cross-linking."
      supports: REFUTE
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      snippet: >-
        No morphological abnormalities were found in the skeleton of Fbln4(-/-)
        mice as compared to wild type littermates except forelimb contractures
        as well as unusually thick collagen fibrils.
      explanation: No murine counterpart of the human prenatal fractures.
- name: Fibulin-4 R/R hypomorphic mouse
  species: Mouse
  genotype: Fibulin-4(R/R) (four-fold reduced expression) and Fibulin-4(+/R) (two-fold)
  publication: PMID:17293478
  description: >-
    A dosage series that survives into postnatal life. Homozygotes have
    ascending aortic dilatation, a tortuous stiffened aorta, thickened aortic
    valve leaflets with stenosis and insufficiency, cystic media degeneration,
    raised TGF-beta signalling and tissue angiotensin II, developmental lung
    emphysema, and death around three months. Heterozygotes acquire alveolar
    breakdown with age and infection and occasionally form small aneurysms.
    This is the line used for the losartan, aliskiren and propranolol
    comparisons.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Aortic Medial Degeneration
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: HIGH
    model_scale: TISSUE
    limitations: >-
      The hypomorphic allele arose incidentally from targeting the adjacent
      Mus81 gene, so it models reduced fibulin-4 dose and not any patient
      allele.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:21858106
      reference_title: "Impaired vascular contractility and aortic wall degeneration in fibulin-4 deficient mice: effect of angiotensin II type 1 (AT1) receptor blockade."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      snippet: >-
        Medial degeneration is a key feature of aneurysm disease and aortic
        dissection.
      explanation: The lesion this line was built to study, matched to human aneurysm pathology.
  - target: Increased TGF-beta Signaling in the Aortic Wall
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: CELLULAR
    limitations: >-
      Smooth muscle cells with complete fibulin-4 loss do not show increased
      TGF-beta activation, so the dose matters and the finding does not
      generalise across genotypes.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:26607280
      reference_title: Fibulin-4 deficiency increases TGF-β signalling in aortic smooth muscle cells due to elevated TGF-β2 levels.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      snippet: >-
        Significantly increased TGF-β2 levels were also detectable in plasma
        from homozygous Fibulin-4(R/R) mice, not in wild type mice.
      explanation: The TGF-beta2 elevation is measurable in plasma, not only in tissue.
  - target: Emphysema
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: TISSUE
    limitations: >-
      Mouse lung alveolarisation differs from human, and the human emphysema
      is variable while the homozygous mouse phenotype is uniform.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:25255451
      reference_title: Extracellular matrix defects in aneurysmal Fibulin-4 mice predispose to lung emphysema.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      snippet: >-
        we demonstrate that aneurysmal Fibulin-4(R/R) mice display severe
        developmental lung emphysema
      explanation: Developmental emphysema at four-fold reduced fibulin-4.
- name: Fbln4 smooth muscle cell knockout mouse
  species: Mouse
  genotype: Fbln4(SMKO) (SM22-Cre conditional deletion in smooth muscle)
  publication: PMID:20019329
  description: >-
    The workhorse line for aortic mechanism and drug work. Deleting fibulin-4
    in smooth muscle gives postnatal ascending aortic aneurysm with an
    immature contractile phenotype, focal proliferation, ERK1/2 activation,
    loss of the elastic lamina to smooth muscle connections by postnatal day
    7, and then the PAR1, Egr1, ACE, angiotensin II, thrombospondin-1 and
    cofilin cascade. Deleting Egr1 or Thbs1, or deleting Fbln4 only after the
    connections have formed, prevents the aneurysm; losartan and captopril
    prevent it when given in the first month.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Impaired Smooth Muscle Cell Contractile Maturation
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: CELLULAR
    limitations: >-
      Deletion is restricted to smooth muscle, so the model says nothing about
      skin, lung, diaphragm or bone, and it is a null allele rather than a
      patient missense allele.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:20019329
      reference_title: "Fibulin-4 deficiency results in ascending aortic aneurysms: a potential link between abnormal smooth muscle cell phenotype and aneurysm progression."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      snippet: >-
        aortic walls fail to fully differentiate, exhibiting reduced expression
        of SM-specific contractile genes and focal proliferation of SMCs
        accompanied by degenerative changes of the medial wall
      explanation: Failed terminal differentiation in both germline and smooth muscle knockouts.
  - target: Loss of Elastic Lamina-Smooth Muscle Cell Connections
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: TISSUE
    description: >-
      Timing is the key result: deleting Fbln4 at postnatal day 7, after the
      connections are made, prevents aneurysm, which makes the developmental
      connection defect necessary rather than incidental.
    limitations: >-
      Conditional timing experiments of this kind have no human counterpart,
      so the necessity claim cannot be tested in patients.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:26486174
      reference_title: Abnormal mechanosensing and cofilin activation promote the progression of ascending aortic aneurysms in mice.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      snippet: >-
        Postnatal deletion of Fbln4 in SMCs at P7 prevented cofilin activation
        and aneurysm formation, suggesting that these processes required
        disruption of elastic lamina-SMC connections.
      explanation: The timing experiment establishing when the lesion must occur.
  - target: PAR1 Activation in Aortic Smooth Muscle Cells
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: CELLULAR
    limitations: >-
      PAR1, Egr1 and thrombin are raised in human thoracic aortic aneurysm

      EFEMP2 patients.

      explanation: How PAR1 entered the cascade, as an unbiased pathway prediction later confirmed.
  - target: Deficient Elastin Cross-Linking
    relationship: FAILS_TO_RECAPITULATE
    fidelity: LOW
    model_scale: TISSUE
    description: >-
      Collagen cross-linking falls in knockout aortas, but elastin
      cross-linking is unaffected and mature lysyl oxidase is maintained,
      which is one half of the unresolved cross-linking conflict.
    limitations: >-

      and the E57K skin does show reduced desmosine.
    evidence:

      reference_title: "Loss of fibulin-4 disrupts collagen synthesis and maturation: implications for pathology resulting from EFEMP2 mutations."
      supports: REFUTE
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      snippet: >-

      explanation: >-

- name: Fbln4 E57K knock-in mouse
  species: Mouse
  genotype: Fbln4(E57K/E57K) (knock-in of the recurrent human p.Glu57Lys allele)
  publication: PMID:26178373
  description: >-
    The only model of an actual patient allele. Homozygotes survive into
    adulthood with loose skin, bent forelimb, aortic aneurysm, arterial
    tortuosity, hypertension and pulmonary emphysema; the mutant protein is
    made but dimerizes, is poorly secreted and triggers ER stress; lysyl
    oxidase maturation is compromised, skin desmosine is reduced and dermal
    and tendon collagen fibrils are abnormal. The arterial lesion is confined
    to conduit vessels, with resistance and muscular arteries spared, and
    arterial elastin cross-linking and total elastin are unchanged.

EFEMP2-Related Cutis Laxa (ARCL1B): research report
claude-haiku-5-5, claude-opus-5-5 6 citations 2026-11-10T18:38:35.959503

EFEMP2-Related Cutis Laxa (ARCL1B): research report

Target: EFEMP2-Related Cutis Laxa · MONDO:0013754 · OMIM:614437 · causal gene EFEMP2 (fibulin-4, alias FBLN4; HGNC:3219; chromosome 11q13) Retrieved: 2026-11-10. Quotes come from PubMed abstracts fetched with NCBI E-utilities, from the GeneReviews abstract served by Europe PMC, and from the local ClinGen export cache/clingen/gene_validity.csv.

1. Disease information

Overview. EFEMP2-related cutis laxa is an autosomal recessive connective tissue disorder caused by biallelic loss-of-function variants in EFEMP2, which encodes the extracellular matrix glycoprotein fibulin-4. Fibulin-4 is needed to build elastic fibers and cross-link them. The main problem is in the large elastic arteries: aortic aneurysm, arterial elongation, tortuosity and stenosis. Lax skin, emphysema, hernias and skeletal fragility vary from patient to patient. Disease ranges from death before or shortly after birth to isolated aortopathy with no skin signs at all.

  • GeneReviews (Loeys, De Paepe, Urban; PMID:21563328; Europe PMC lists first publication and last revision both as 2023-06-15): "EFEMP2-related cutis laxa, or autosomal recessive cutis laxa type 1B (ARCL1B), is characterized by cutis laxa and systemic involvement, most commonly arterial tortuosity, aneurysms, and stenosis; retrognathia; joint laxity; and arachnodactyly. Severity ranges from perinatal lethality as a result of cardiopulmonary failure to manifestations limited to the vascular and craniofacial systems."
  • MONDO definition (OLS4): "An autosomal recessive cutis laxa type I characterized by disturbed elastic fiber formation resulting in severe systemic connective tissue abnormalities that has material basis in homozygous or compound heterozygous mutation in the EFEMP2 gene on chromosome 11q13."

Identifiers

System ID Status
MONDO MONDO:0013754 "cutis laxa, autosomal recessive, type 1B" verified (OLS4)
OMIM OMIM:614437 verified (MONDO xref)
DOID DOID:0070133 verified (MONDO xref)
GARD GARD:0015804 verified (MONDO xref)
MedGen / UMLS MEDGEN:482428 / UMLS:C3280798 verified (MONDO xref)
GeneReviews PMID:21563328 (NBK54469) PMID verified; the NBK URL returned a browser-check page, so I haven't confirmed it
Orphanet Probably filed under Autosomal recessive cutis laxa type 1 (ORPHA:90350) not verified: search returned no Orphanet page
ICD-10 Q82.8 (other specified congenital malformations of skin) is the usual code for congenital cutis laxa not verified
ClinGen EFEMP2 / MONDO:0013754, AR, Definitive, Hereditary Cardiovascular Disease GCEP, 2024-08-23, CGGV:assertion_8911130f-a1fc-4404-8905-5632a49a95fe-2024-08-23T160000.000Z verified (local ClinGen CSV)

Synonyms: ARCL1B; cutis laxa, autosomal recessive, type IB; fibulin-4 deficiency; FBLN4-related cutis laxa. A recessive aortopathy is also described with no skin signs, sometimes called "recessively inherited aortic aneurysm" (PMID:22440127). The Kerala cohort was described as a "lethal arteriopathy syndrome" (PMID:22943132).

Source type. The evidence is aggregated case reports and case series, roughly 60–80 published individuals by 2024. The 22-infant Kerala series and the 9-patient Saudi family series are the largest. There is no EHR-derived or registry data.

2. Etiology

  • Cause. Biallelic (homozygous or compound heterozygous) pathogenic EFEMP2 variants. The condition is monogenic and fully genetic.
  • Genetic risk factors. Consanguinity is the main risk factor. Founder alleles drive local clusters: p.Asp203Ala in South India (PMID:22943132) and p.Glu161Lys in Saudi Arabia (PMID:22440127, PMID:39489349).
  • Modifier genes. EFEMP2 itself has been proposed as a modifier. A heterozygous fibulin-4 variant was suggested to modify the phenotype in a family with recessive ELN cutis laxa (PMID:19194475; this is a title-level claim and I have not read the abstract). No modifier of ARCL1B itself is established.
  • Environmental risk and protective factors. None is documented. GeneReviews recommends avoiding sun tanning (skin) and cigarette smoking (emphysema). These are tertiary-prevention measures, not etiologic factors.
  • Gene-environment interaction. Not studied in humans. Mouse work shows that mechanical stretch and angiotensin II drive aneurysm signaling, and fibulin-4 loss makes smooth muscle cells more sensitive to both (PMID:30355232). That makes hemodynamic load a plausible but unproven modifier.

3. Phenotypes

Frequencies come from the Kerala p.Asp203Ala cohort (n=22; PMID:22943132) unless stated otherwise. Other cohorts differ, and in some, such as the Saudi p.Glu161Lys families, skin laxity is absent. The HPO IDs below are suggestions recalled from memory and must be looked up before binding.

Phenotype Suggested HPO Onset / course Frequency / notes Key PMID
Ascending aortic aneurysm / aortic root dilatation HP:0004970 Ascending tortuous aorta?; HP:0002616 Aortic root aneurysm; HP:0004942 Aortic aneurysm congenital to infancy, progressive near-universal; Z-scores 5–33 (PMID:22440127) 16685658, 20389311, 22440127
Arterial tortuosity / elongation HP:0005116 Arterial tortuosity congenital very frequent 22943132, 22070778, 23212998
Arterial / pulmonary artery stenosis or occlusion HP:0100545 Arterial stenosis; HP:0004415 Pulmonary artery stenosis neonatal frequent; occlusion was fatal at 27 days (PMID:17937443) 17937443, 20389311
Aortic isthmus hypoplasia HP:0012304 Hypoplastic aortic arch? congenital 9/22; "correlated with early death (≤4 months)" 22943132

Key quotes: - PMID:16685658: "She had multiple bone fractures at birth and was diagnosed with cutis laxa, vascular tortuosity, ascending aortic aneurysm, developmental emphysema, inguinal and diaphragmatic hernia, joint laxity, and pectus excavatum by age 2 years." - PMID:22943132: "The phenotype included a variable combination of cutis laxa (52%), long philtrum-thin vermillion (90%), micrognathia (43%), hypertelorism (57%), prominent eyes (43%), sagging cheeks (43%), long slender digits (48%), and visible arterial pulsations (38%)." - PMID:20389311: "patients with recessive FBLN4 mutations are predominantly characterized by aortic aneurysms, arterial tortuosity and stenosis." - PMID:22440127: "The skin manifestations of cutis laxa were not found in any patient." - PMID:30140196 (fetal): "Anomalies included cutis laxa, arachnodactyly, clubfoot, wormian bones, moderate bowing of long bones with slender bone trabeculae, rib fractures, undermuscularized diaphragm, hiatal hernia, and arterial tortuosity with thick vascular walls and disorganized elastic fibers."

Laboratory and histological findings. Skin elastic fibers are underdeveloped, sparse or fragmented (PMID:16685658, PMID:17937443). The aortic media shows cystic medial degeneration and elastic fiber fragmentation (PMID:19664000, PMID:42404516). Patient fibroblasts and aortic wall carry less fibulin-4, and pSmad2/CTGF staining is increased (PMID:20389311).

Quality of life. No formal QoL studies exist (EQ-5D, SF-36 and similar). The burden comes from repeated cardiovascular surgery, airway compromise and lifelong imaging surveillance.

4. Genetic / molecular information

  • Gene. EFEMP2 (fibulin-4; HGNC:3219; NCBI Gene 30008, not verified). 11q13. It is a short fibulin with tandem calcium-binding EGF-like (cbEGF) domains and a C-terminal fibulin domain.
  • Inheritance. Autosomal recessive. ClinGen rates the gene-disease pair AR Definitive (2024-08-23).
  • Variant classes (all germline):
  • Missense, the most common class. Examples: p.Glu57Lys (c.169G>A), the first and a recurrent allele (PMID:16685658). p.Glu126Lys, p.Ala397Thr, p.Glu126Val (PMID:20389311). p.Cys267Tyr (PMID:19664000). p.Arg279Cys (PMID:17937443). p.Glu161Lys, the Saudi founder (PMID:22440127). p.Asp203Ala (c.608A>C), the South Indian founder (PMID:22943132). p.Arg227Cys. p.Tyr155Ser (c.464A>C) (PMID:39764439).
  • Frameshift: c.1070_1073dupCCGC, subject to NMD (PMID:17937443); c.577delC (PMID:20389311).
  • Nonsense: c.639C>A p.Cys213* (PMID:30140196); a homozygous premature stop codon in MZ twins (PMID:22070778).
  • Functional consequence: loss of function, by several routes:
  • Impaired secretion or ER retention: E57K dimerizes, is inefficiently secreted and triggers ER stress (PMID:26178373). E126K and C267Y also impair secretion (PMID:27339457).
  • Disrupted binding to partners: E126K binds collagen IV, fibrillin-1, LTBP1 and LTBP4 less well. A397T loses LTBP1 binding. E57K reduces binding to the LOX propeptide (PMID:27339457).
  • Null alleles through NMD (PMID:17937443).
  • PMID:27339457: "different mutations in the fibulin-4 gene result in different molecular defects affecting secretion rates, protein stability, LOX-induced cross-linking, or binding to other ECM components and molecules of the TGF-β pathway".
  • Genotype-phenotype. Truncating alleles tend toward fetal or perinatal lethality (PMID:30140196, PMID:22070778). p.Asp203Ala homozygosity is usually lethal (17/21 died, median age 4 months). p.Glu161Lys gives aortopathy without skin laxity and has survivors into adulthood. These are cohort-level observations; no formal correlation study has been done.
  • Heterozygous / dominant claims (disputed). A heterozygous p.Arg83Cys was reported in a family with dominant nonsyndromic thoracic aortic aneurysm, based on in-silico evidence only (PMID:38113391). Treat this as unconfirmed. ClinGen's Definitive call is for AR only. In mouse, a modest dose reduction (Fibulin-4+/R) "occasionally resulted in small aneurysm formation" (PMID:17293478).
  • Allele frequency. Not retrieved from gnomAD (not checked). The founder alleles are expected to be enriched in Kerala and Saudi populations.
  • Epigenetics and chromosomal abnormalities. None reported.

5. Environmental information

Not applicable as a cause: there are no known toxins, infectious agents or lifestyle triggers. Advice to avoid smoking and sun tanning is for managing complications (GeneReviews).

6. Mechanism / pathophysiology

Ordered causal chain

  1. Biallelic EFEMP2 loss-of-function variants reduce or remove functional extracellular fibulin-4, through NMD, ER retention or secretion failure, or loss of binding (PMID:17937443, PMID:26178373, PMID:27339457). Demonstrated in patient fibroblasts and recombinant protein.
  2. Lysyl oxidase (LOX) is not recruited or activated properly. Fibulin-4 binds the LOX propeptide and tethers LOX to tropoelastin (PMID:19855011). It is also needed to transfer copper from ATP7A to LOX in the trans-Golgi network, and without that transfer the LTQ cofactor does not form (PMID:33239290). LOX processing is impaired in E57K skin and in Fbln4−/− bone (PMID:26178373, PMID:26690653). Demonstrated in mouse and in vitro.
  3. Elastin and collagen cross-linking falls, and elastic fiber assembly is defective. Null mice "do not develop intact elastic fibers but contain irregular elastin aggregates" and "elastin cross-links in fibulin-4-/- tissues were largely diminished" (PMID:16478991). Collagen fibrils become abnormal in aorta, skin, tendon and bone (PMID:26220971, PMID:26178373, PMID:26690653).
  4. Caveat: in Fbln4 E57K mice, elastin cross-linking was normal in arteries (PMID:28508064), and in SMKO aorta elastin cross-linking was unaffected (PMID:26220971). So reduced LOX-mediated cross-linking is not the whole vascular mechanism.
  5. Branch A (connective tissues): fragmented, sparse elastic fibers lead to cutis laxa (dermis), emphysema (lung parenchyma), hernias and joint laxity. Defective collagen cross-linking leads to bone fragility, fractures and tendon contractures (PMID:26690653, PMID:26711913).
  6. Branch B (aortic wall). Smooth muscle cells lose their connections to the elastic laminae, and the elastin-contractile unit breaks down (PMID:26486174, PMID:30355232).
  7. The smooth muscle cells then fail to differentiate fully. Contractile gene expression falls, they proliferate, and ERK1/2 signaling rises (PMID:20019329). Mouse.
  8. Abnormal mechanosensing sets off a signaling cascade. Thrombin, MMP-9 and stretch activate PAR1, which induces Egr1 (PMID:32580633). Egr1 induces ACE, which drives local angiotensin II signaling (PMID:23636094) and induces Thbs1 (PMID:30355232). AngII acts through PI3K to raise SSH1, which activates cofilin and remodels the actin cytoskeleton (PMID:26486174). Mouse, with PAR1/Egr1/thrombin and Thbs1 confirmed in human TAA tissue.
  9. TGF-β signaling rises (pSmad2, CTGF; TGF-β2 especially). This is shown in patient aortic and lung tissue (PMID:20389311) and in mouse smooth muscle cells (PMID:26607280, PMID:17293478). Downstream effects include lower PGC1α, reduced mitochondrial respiration and more ROS (PMID:29931197), and smooth muscle senescence with a SASP (PMID:38902222). Whether TGF-β is a driver or a compensatory change is unresolved.
  10. Medial degeneration and aneurysm follow. Cystic medial degeneration, elastic fiber fragmentation and smooth muscle loss (PMID:21858106) produce ascending aortic aneurysm, arterial elongation and tortuosity, and stenosis. Ultimately rupture, dissection or cardiopulmonary failure can occur. Hypertension and aortic valve disease add to the load (PMID:28508064, PMID:17293478, PMID:36565192).
  11. The conduit-artery selectivity is explained by FBLN4 being required mainly in large elastic arteries, while vessel wall structure in muscular arteries is normal (PMID:28508064).

Key quotes: - PMID:19855011: "These data suggest a mechanism of elastogenesis, in which a sufficient amount of FBLN4 is essential for tethering LOX to tropoelastin to facilitate cross-linking." - PMID:33239290: "Our studies showed that fibulin-4 is required for copper ion transfer from the copper transporter ATP7A to LOX in the trans-Golgi network (TGN), which is a necessary step for LTQ formation." - PMID:20389311: "pSmad2 and CTGF immunostaining of aortic and lung tissue revealed an increase in transforming growth factor (TGF)beta signaling." - PMID:26486174: "These results suggest that aneurysm formation arises from abnormal mechanosensing of SMCs resulting from the loss of elastic lamina-SMC connections and from increased SSH1 and cofilin activity"

Suggested ontology terms (all unverified; look each up before binding)

  • GO BP: elastic fiber assembly (GO:0048251); collagen fibril organization (GO:0030199); peptidyl-lysine oxidation / protein-lysine 6-oxidase activity of LOX (GO:0004720, MF); transforming growth factor beta receptor signaling pathway (GO:0007179); ERK1 and ERK2 cascade (GO:0070371); angiotensin-activated signaling pathway (GO:0038166); smooth muscle cell differentiation (GO:0051145); response to endoplasmic reticulum stress (GO:0034976); cellular senescence (GO:0090398); actin cytoskeleton organization (GO:0030036); copper ion transport (GO:0006825).
  • GO CC: elastic fiber (GO:0071953); extracellular matrix (GO:0031012); trans-Golgi network (GO:0005802).
  • CL: aortic smooth muscle cell (CL:0002539); vascular smooth muscle cell (CL:0000359); fibroblast of dermis (CL:0002551); endothelial cell of aorta?; osteoblast (CL:0000062); chondrocyte.
  • CHEBI: desmosine; copper(2+); angiotensin II; losartan (CHEBI:6541?).

Molecular profiling

Aortic transcriptomes and proteomes are published for Fibulin-4+/R and R/R mice (PMID:17293478, PMID:29931197), SMKO aortas from P1 to P30 (PMID:26486174), late-embryonic and newborn aorta arrays (PMID:30312140) and DKO valve RNA-seq (PMID:36565192). No human omics, single-cell or CRISPR-screen data were found. I did not search GEO accessions.

7. Anatomical structures affected

  • Primary: ascending aorta and aortic root (UBERON:0001496 ascending aorta), aortic arch and isthmus, pulmonary trunk and arteries, carotid, iliac and other elastic conduit arteries; dermis (UBERON:0002067); lung parenchyma and alveoli.
  • Secondary: heart (left ventricular dilation and aortic regurgitation in mice; valve thickening), diaphragm (hernia, undermuscularized), skeleton (long bones, ribs, skull fractures), tendons and joints, inguinal canal, cornea (one case), upper airway (compression, retrognathia).
  • Systems: cardiovascular (dominant), integumentary, respiratory, musculoskeletal, craniofacial.
  • Subcellular: extracellular matrix and elastic fiber; ER (retention of mutant protein); trans-Golgi network (copper loading of LOX).
  • Laterality: systemic and bilateral.

8. Temporal development

  • Onset: congenital, often detectable prenatally from the second trimester, with tortuous dilated aorta, pulmonary atresia, fractures and clubfoot (PMID:30140196, PMID:38923535). In the Kerala cohort, median age at presentation was 1.5 months. A milder group comes to attention in childhood or is found as an asymptomatic adult on family screening (PMID:22440127; median age of onset of thoracic aortic dilatation 4.5 years in the Saudi pediatric cohort, PMID:39489349).
  • Course: lifelong. The aortic aneurysm progresses, and redo surgery is often needed (PMID:36518920, PMID:24276535).
  • Critical window: in mice, angiotensin blockade works only "within a narrow therapeutic window during the first month of life" (PMID:23636094). Prenatal losartan prevented elastic fiber fragmentation, while postnatal losartan improved survival without fixing wall structure (PMID:21858106). Whether a similar window exists in humans is unknown.
  • Remission: none.

9. Inheritance and population

  • Pattern: autosomal recessive (HP:0000007). Penetrance appears complete for biallelic genotypes, but expressivity varies widely, including within families (PMID:22440127: "Intrafamilial variability was also evident"). Heterozygous carriers are asymptomatic according to GeneReviews. The dominant claim in PMID:38113391 is unconfirmed.
  • Prevalence: not established; ultra-rare. Literature cases number in the dozens, likely fewer than 100 (my estimate from the case literature, not a sourced figure). Orphanet prevalence was not retrieved.
  • Founder effects: p.Asp203Ala in South India (Kerala): "Genetic studies revealed an identical c.608A > C (p. Asp203Ala) mutation in exon 7 of the FBLN4 gene in all 22 patients, homozygous in 21". p.Glu161Lys in Saudi Arabia: a homozygous EFEMP2 variant was found in 6 of 13 genetically solved childhood TAD families (46.2%; PMID:39489349).
  • Consanguinity: common. 8 of 22 Kerala families; all 4 Saudi families in PMID:22440127.
  • Sex ratio: 11 males and 11 females in the Kerala cohort; no sex bias is expected.
  • Carrier frequency: not established (gnomAD not checked).

10. Diagnostics

  • Clinical criteria: none formalized. GeneReviews: "The diagnosis of EFEMP2-related cutis laxa is established in a proband with suggestive findings and biallelic pathogenic variants in EFEMP2 identified by molecular genetic testing."
  • Imaging: echocardiography (aortic root and ascending aorta Z-scores), CT or MR angiography head to pelvis to map tortuosity, stenosis and aneurysms (radiologic phenotype described in PMID:24838734), and prenatal ultrasound (PMID:30140196, PMID:38923535).
  • Histology: skin biopsy shows reduced or fragmented elastic fibers. Aortic specimens show medial degeneration and elastic fiber fragmentation.
  • Fibroblast studies (research use): reduced fibulin-4 in the medium or ECM and increased pSmad2 (PMID:20389311, PMID:17937443).
  • Genetic testing: multigene panels for cutis laxa or heritable thoracic aortic disease (EFEMP2, FBLN5, LTBP4, ELN, ATP6V0A2, PYCR1, ALDH18A1, ATP7A, FBN1, TGFBR1/2, SMAD3, SLC2A10 and others), or exome/genome sequencing. Exome sequencing solved delayed or misdiagnosed cases (PMID:39764439, where the child was first misdiagnosed with Takayasu arteritis) and prenatal cases (PMID:38923535). Targeted founder-variant testing makes sense in Kerala and Saudi populations. CMA, karyotype, mtDNA and repeat testing have no role.
  • Differential diagnosis:
  • Arterial tortuosity syndrome (SLC2A10): tortuosity without emphysema, normal elastogenesis genes.
  • FBLN5-related ARCL1A and LTBP4-related ARCL1C: "FBLN5 and LTBP4 mutations cause a very similar phenotype associated with severe pulmonary emphysema, in the absence of vascular tortuosity or aneurysms" (PMID:22829427).
  • ELN-related cutis laxa (AD and AR).
  • Loeys-Dietz syndrome (TGFBR1/2, SMAD3, TGFB2/3).
  • Marfan syndrome (FBN1).
  • EMILIN1 arterial tortuosity-bone fragility syndrome (PMID:36351433).
  • Osteogenesis imperfecta, for the fracture-predominant fetal presentation (PMID:22070778).
  • ARCL2 / ATP6V0A2, PYCR1, ALDH18A1 forms; occipital horn syndrome / ATP7A; LTBP1-related cutis laxa (PMID:33991472).
  • Takayasu arteritis, as an acquired mimic (PMID:39764439).
  • Screening: cascade carrier testing; prenatal diagnosis and PGT when familial variants are known; echocardiographic screening of siblings (PMID:22440127 found affected sibs aged 17 and 20 this way). No newborn screening exists.

11. Outcome / prognosis

  • Mortality: high in early-onset and founder-lethal forms. Kerala: "Homozygosity was lethal (17/21 died, median age 4 months). Isthmic hypoplasia (n = 9) correlated with early death (≤4 months)." Perinatal deaths and terminations are reported for truncating alleles (PMID:19664000, PMID:22070778, PMID:30140196, PMID:17937443).
  • Survivors: longer-term survival after surgery is reported (PMID:23532871). In the Saudi childhood-TAD cohort (PMID:39489349) all patients were alive at 3–31 years. That cohort mixes genes and is a high-intervention setting.
  • Complications: aneurysm rupture or dissection, airway compression, respiratory failure from emphysema, need for redo aortic surgery, baroreflex failure (PMID:24733866), cerebral vasospasm.
  • Prognostic factors: isthmic hypoplasia; genotype (truncating or p.Asp203Ala more severe than p.Glu161Lys); prenatal presentation. No biomarkers have been validated.

12. Treatment

No disease-specific therapy exists, and no trials were found on ClinicalTrials.gov (I didn't run a separate search, but none turned up in the literature).

GeneReviews management (verbatim): "Treatment of aortic root dilatation with beta-blockers or angiotensin receptor inhibitors can be considered. Aortic aneurysm replacement has been performed successfully. Symptomatic treatment of pulmonary emphysema; muscle-reinforcing physical therapy for joint hypermobility; routine repair of hernias. Tracheostomy may be necessary when retrognathia leads to upper-airway obstruction."

Experimental and preclinical targets (mouse only): PI3K inhibitors (PMID:26486174), Thbs1 deletion (PMID:30355232), Egr1/PAR1 (PMID:32580633), PGC1α activation (PMID:29931197), JAK/STAT or TGF-β inhibition and senolytics in vitro (PMID:38902222). No gene therapy exists.

13. Prevention

  • Primary: carrier testing, prenatal diagnosis and PGT in at-risk families (GeneReviews). Founder-variant awareness in Kerala and Saudi populations.
  • Secondary: echocardiographic screening of siblings.
  • Tertiary (GeneReviews): "Follow-up evaluations with a cardiologist and pulmonologist at least annually starting from the time of diagnosis. Annual MR angiography from head to pelvis. Agents/circumstances to avoid: Sun tanning to avoid damaging the skin; cigarette smoking to avoid worsening of emphysema."
  • Counseling: "each sib of an affected individual has at conception a 25% chance of being affected, a 50% chance of being an asymptomatic carrier, and a 25% chance of being unaffected and not a carrier."
  • No vaccines or public-health measures apply.

14. Other species / natural disease

No naturally occurring animal EFEMP2 disease was found (I didn't search OMIA directly). EFEMP2 is conserved across vertebrates. The mouse ortholog is Efemp2 (Fbln4); I haven't confirmed the MGI or NCBI Gene IDs. No zoonotic relevance.

15. Model organisms (all Mus musculus, NCBITaxon:10090)

Model Recapitulates Limitations PMID
Fbln4−/− (germline null) no intact elastic fibers, emphysema, arterial tortuosity, aneurysm, rupture; forelimb contractures; abnormal collagen and LOX activation in bone perinatal lethal, so postnatal course can't be studied; more severe than most human missense genotypes 16478991, 26690653, 26711913, 33239290
Fibulin-4 R/R hypomorph (4-fold reduced) and +/R (2-fold) ascending aortic dilatation, tortuous stiff aorta, valve stenosis/insufficiency, cystic medial degeneration, raised TGF-β, death around 3 months; emphysema the hypomorph came from targeting Mus81, so it is a dosage model, not a patient allele 17293478, 21858106, 25255451, 29931197, 38902222
Fbln4 SMKO (SM22-Cre) postnatal ascending aortic aneurysm; smooth muscle immaturity; ACE/AngII, Egr1, Thbs1 and cofilin pathways; losartan/captopril prevention smooth muscle–restricted, so no skin, lung or bone phenotype 20019329, 23636094, 26486174, 30355232, 32580633
Fbln4 E57K knock-in (human allele) survives to adulthood with loose skin, bent forelimb, aortic aneurysm, tortuosity, emphysema, hypertension; ER stress; abnormal collagen fibrils; conduit-artery selectivity arterial elastin cross-linking normal, which conflicts with the LOX model 26178373, 28508064, 37267118
Fbln4 EC + SMC double KO worse aneurysm, aortic valve thickening, EndMT genetic overlay with no direct patient counterpart 36565192
Fbln4 dose series (SM-Cre graded) dose-dependent stiffness, then aneurysm — 19855011

PMID:26178373: "The mutant mice survived into adulthood and displayed abnormalities in multiple organ systems, including loose skin, bent forelimb, aortic aneurysm, tortuous artery, and pulmonary emphysema."

The E57K knock-in is the best model of a patient allele. The SMKO line is the workhorse for aneurysm mechanism and drug work. No zebrafish, iPSC or organoid models were found. Human aortic endothelial knockdown (PMID:36565192) and HEK293 recombinant mutant studies (PMID:27339457) are the in-vitro systems.

Gaps and cautions for curation

  1. The LOX cross-linking model conflicts across studies. PMID:16478991 reports elastin cross-links "largely diminished" in null mice. PMID:28508064 and PMID:26220971 found arterial elastin cross-linking normal. Worth a KNOWLEDGE_GAP or two-sided evidence on the LOX node.
  2. TGF-β: driver or marker? The rise is documented in humans and mice, but a causal role isn't shown. Avoid TRIGGERS-style predicates.
  3. Nearly all mechanism evidence downstream of the elastic fiber defect is mouse (MODEL_ORGANISM). Human support covers only the elastic fiber defect, reduced fibulin-4 and pSmad2/CTGF.
  4. The dominant-TAA report (PMID:38113391) rests on in-silico evidence. If it is included, a DISPUTED genetic row fits better than a subtype.
  5. Not verified, so don't bind as written: the Orphanet code, ICD codes, every HPO/GO/CL/CHEBI ID in the tables above, NCBI Gene and MGI IDs, gnomAD frequencies, and the NBK accession.
  6. Next steps: the draft kb/disorders/EFEMP2-Related_Cutis_Laxa.yaml is a stub (23 lines). Its association: Causative field is not a schema slot; the schema uses relationship_type: CAUSATIVE. Cited PMIDs need just fetch-reference. Only PMID:22829427 and PMID:23954411 are already in references_cache/.
